[1]. [*Universal electrical resistivity in iron-based high-temperature superconductors*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23865 "Universal electrical resistivity in iron-based high-temperature superconductors")

E. F. Talantsev

arXiv:2610.23865v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Temperature-dependent electrical resistivity $\rho(T)$ in cuprate, iron-based, and nickelate superconductors represents one of unresolved problems in modern solid-state physics, because of the high absolute value of $\rho(T)$, which, in many cases, exceeds the Ioffe-Rigel criterion, and a large difference in the $\rho(T)$ shape for samples with different doping. Here I show that the nearest neighbors parallel resistivity model (arXiv: 2607.23484) can well describe the in-plane resistivity $\rho_{ab}(T)$ in iron-based superconductors (IBS) of type 11, 111, 122, 1111, 1144 and 42214 in which the spin density wave phase or nematic order is suppressed by doping. The model is based on the postulate that $\rho_{ab}(T)$ is a sum of two parallel conduction channels, where one channel is described by the Bloch-Gr\"uneisen equation and the other by the Arrhenius hopping equation. Consequently, the variety of $\rho_{ab}(T)$ in the IBS is explained as a variation of the Debye temperature $\Theta_D$ and the hopping activation energy $E_a$. The derived Debye temperatures were used to estimate the electron-phonon coupling constant $\lambda_{e-ph}$in the studied IBS.



[2]. [*Resonant suppression of the $\pi$-type Josephson component in a Kitaev-ladder junction*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23875 "Resonant suppression of the $\pi$-type Josephson component in a Kitaev-ladder junction")

Cheng-Rong Xie, Hiroki Tsuchiura, Yukio Tanaka

arXiv:2610.23875v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate Josephson transport through a finite normal segment connecting two Kitaev-ladder superconductors and show that discrete-level detuning controls the sign of the equilibrium Josephson coupling through a branch-selection mechanism. Away from resonance, detuning separates two competing low-energy branches, allowing inter-leg hybridization to clamp the $0$-phase-favoring branch while leaving the $\pi$-favoring branch dispersive. The resulting negative first harmonic evolves from a branch-switching response at weak coupling into a smooth $\pi$-junction regime. At resonance the two clamping thresholds coincide, so the finite interval in which only one branch remains dispersive disappears. Once the inter-leg coupling exceeds the resonant clamping threshold, both low-energy branches become independent of the Josephson phase. The remaining finite-energy states then generate a $0$-type Josephson coupling. At $\phi=\pi$, the helicity decomposition yields an exact clamping identity for arbitrary finite normal-segment length. Within the controlled sweet-point finite-segment theory, serial propagation fixes the off-resonant fourth-order harmonic to be negative, whereas an isolated resonance leaves a positive residual harmonic within the resonant clamping regime. Numerical calculations demonstrate that the resonance-induced suppression persists away from the solvable phase choice. Thus normal-level resonance does not merely enhance Josephson transmission; it changes which low-energy branches contribute to the Josephson coupling.



[3]. [*Multiple propagating modes in random polycrystals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24047 "Multiple propagating modes in random polycrystals")

Ming Huang, Stanislav I. Rokhlin, Michael J. S. Lowe

arXiv:2610.24047v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Multiple-scattering theory predicts that the coherent elastic wave in a statistically isotropic polycrystal is not a single wave. Its dispersion equation admits multiple roots of a given polarization, but these extra modes have never been observed. Here, using grain-scale finite-element simulations of three-dimensional polycrystals, we resolve the coherent field in the frequency--wavenumber plane and directly observe the predicted multiplicity for both transverse and longitudinal excitations: a single mode at low frequencies gives way to two in the geometric regime as the wavelength approaches the grain correlation length. The measured modes follow the roots of the dispersion equation, which always carries a third root whose attenuation exceeds that of the others. It is this attenuation hierarchy, not the root count, that dictates how many modes propagate and can be observed. Coherent wave propagation in random media is thus inherently multimodal in the geometric regime, which we reach here for the first time in polycrystals.



[4]. [*Two quantum spin liquid states coexisting in the kagome antiferromagnet herbertsmithite*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24065 "Two quantum spin liquid states coexisting in the kagome antiferromagnet herbertsmithite")

Arthur C. Campello, Shengtao Jiang, Wei He, Jiajia Wen, Daniel M. Pajerowski, Steven A. Kivelson, Hong-Chen Jiang, Young S. Lee

arXiv:2610.24065v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Herbertsmithite (ZnCu$_3$(OH)$_6$Cl$_2$) is one of the most extensively studied quantum spin liquid (QSL) candidates, yet the precise nature of its ground state remains unresolved. Here, we compare the results of high-resolution inelastic neutron scattering on single crystals of herbertsmithite (Zn$_\mathrm{x}$Cu$_\mathrm{4-x}$(OD)$_6$Cl$_2$, $x\simeq 0.85$) with large-scale density-matrix renormalization group (DMRG) and dynamical DMRG simulations to benchmark the spin Hamiltonian and uncover fundamental properties of the ground state. The measured spin correlations determine an energy density for the Heisenberg model that closely matches simulations, an essential verification of the Hamiltonian. In parallel, DMRG simulations reveal two nearly degenerate QSL states. Both preserve translational symmetry, but one retains the lattice point-group symmetries whereas the other develops anisotropic spin correlations that break point-group symmetry. These two QSLs have distinct spin structure factors, and, remarkably, close agreement with the neutron spectra is achieved with an admixture of the two states. Our results establish a quantitative correspondence between neutron scattering and simulations, and suggest that herbertsmithite realizes a regime in which two proximate gapped QSL states coexist.



[5]. [*Monolayer van der Waals kagome lattice for high-temperature flat-band physics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24071 "Monolayer van der Waals kagome lattice for high-temperature flat-band physics")

Hyungkun Bong, Choongjae Won, Seongyong Lee, Soomin Oh, Phuong X. Nguyen, Raghav Chaturvedi, Sehyun Oh, Jiyong Han, Paul Malinowski, Chad Mowers, Jimin Kim, Sae Hee Ryu, Hyunjong Lee, Jinhyeok Choi, Sangsu Yer, Chris Jozwiak, Aaron Bostwick, Eli Rotenberg, Craig M. Polley, Matthew D. Watson, Timur K. Kim, Heejun Yang, Jieun Lee, Kin Fai Mak, Jie Shan, Jae-Hoon Park, Gil Young Cho, Kyle M. Shen, Mingu Kang

arXiv:2610.24071v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Kagome lattice materials host a remarkably rich landscape of novel quantum phenomena, all arising from the intrinsic interplay of topology, correlation, and frustration. While previous studies have been mostly limited to bulk three-dimensional (3D) kagome crystals, the realization of a true two-dimensional (2D) kagome system has remained a long-standing goal, to unlock fundamentally new physics and engineering pathways inaccessible in 3D systems. In this work, we establish the first ideal 2D monolayer kagome lattices by leveraging a novel and versatile van der Waals (vdW) kagome family, A2T3X4 (A = K, Rb, Cs; T = Ni, Pd, Pt; X = S, Se). Our nano-focused angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (nano-ARPES) directly characterizes the electronic structure of A2T3X4 down to the monolayer limit, notably revealing an isolated kagome-derived flat band just below the Fermi level. Via gate-engineering of 2D A2T3X4, the kagome flat band can be further tuned to the Fermi level, allowing direct access to the partially filled flat band physics and establishing a qualitatively new 2D flat band platform. Compared to existing moire-based artificial flat band systems, this vdW kagome-based intrinsic flat band system realizes more than 220 times higher flat band electron density, opening an entirely new density and energy regime for exploring 2D flat band physics. Harnessing this high-electron-density flat band in 2D vdW kagome system provides a clear pathway to scale emergent flat band phenomena - such as fractional quantum anomalous Hall states - to far higher temperatures than currently achievable, as long-envisioned in theory and further supported by our many-body calculations.



[6]. [*Quantifying the Kinetic and Thermodynamic Origins of the Glass-Forming Ability in Metals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24101 "Quantifying the Kinetic and Thermodynamic Origins of the Glass-Forming Ability in Metals")

Weiwei Jin, Jinpeng Fan, Mark D. Shattuck, Corey S. O'Hern

arXiv:2610.24101v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The glass-forming ability (GFA) of a metallic liquid can be quantified by the critical cooling rate $R_c$, below which it crystallizes upon cooling. $R_c$ is determined by the competition between $R$ and the crystallization rate $\mathcal{K}_{\rm X}$. Here, we calculate $R_c$ and $\mathcal{K}_{\rm X}$ in model pure metals and alloys with BCC/B2 and FCC equilibrium crystal structures. Using classical nucleation theory and independently calculated crystal growth rates, we evaluate $\mathcal{K}_{\rm X}=(IU^3)^{1/4}$ at $T=0.8T_m$, where $I$ and $U$ are the nucleation and growth rates and $T_m$ is the melting temperature. We find a strong positive correlation between $\mathcal{K}_{\rm X}$ and $R_c$ over eleven orders of magnitude. We decompose $\mathcal{K}_{\rm X}$ into a product of a kinetic contribution, involving atomic cluster attachment and diffusion, and a thermodynamic contribution. We show that the thermodynamic contribution to $\mathcal{K}_{\rm X}$ dominates the kinetic contribution over the range of cooling rates that we consider. The nucleation barrier $\Delta G_c \sim \tilde\gamma^3/\Delta\tilde\mu^2$, where $\tilde\gamma$ is the reduced effective liquid--crystal interfacial free energy and $\Delta\tilde\mu$ is the reduced bulk driving force, determines the magnitude of the thermodynamic contribution. The relative importance of $\tilde\gamma$ and $\Delta\tilde\mu$ depends on the material: a large interfacial free energy can suppress nucleation in some systems, whereas a weak bulk driving force can inhibit crystallization in others. Analyses of the structural and chemical order show that better glass formers have enhanced structural frustration, more icosahedral-like and less crystal-like local environments, and a larger chemical-order mismatch between the supercooled liquid and crystal phases.



[7]. [*Gate-defined quantum dot and charge noise analysis in a Forksheet transistor*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24136 "Gate-defined quantum dot and charge noise analysis in a Forksheet transistor")

Dominic Leclerc, Claude Rohrbacher, Alexis Morel, Joffrey Rivard, Romain Ritzenthaler, Christian Lupien, Hans Mertens, Naoto Horiguchi, Eva Dupont-Ferrier

arXiv:2610.24136v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We report the cryogenic characterization of a 14-nm nMOS Forksheet transistor down to 10 mK. We demonstrate that the device retains transistor operation with improved characteristics at cryogenic temperatures, while also forming a gate-defined quantum dot that can be tuned to the single-electron regime. In the quantum-dot regime, we perform low-frequency charge-noise measurements across different charge occupations and observe an average charge-noise amplitude of $\langle S_0\rangle = 10\;\mu\mathrm{eV}/\sqrt{\mathrm{Hz}}$ at 1 Hz, with a minimum measured value of $3.3\;\mu\mathrm{eV}/\sqrt{\mathrm{Hz}}$. These results highlight the potential of Forksheet technology for the monolithic cointegration of quantum and classical CMOS functionalities in future quantum processors.



[8]. [*Boundary Element Modeling for THz-SNOM Near-Field Probes*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24151 "Boundary Element Modeling for THz-SNOM Near-Field Probes")

Noah Reich, Richard Kim, Thomas Koschny, Jigang Wang

arXiv:2610.24151v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Terahertz scanning near-field optical microscopy (THz-SNOM) enables spatially and spectrally resolved investigation of low-energy collective and topological states in quantum materials and nano-structured devices by exploiting the strongly localized near field generated by a polarized atomic force microscopy (AFM) tip. Because the THz near-field response is highly sensitive to tip geometry, understanding how geometric parameters control field confinement and decay is essential for selecting tip designs that provide the spatial resolution required for specific experiments. In this work, we develop a boundary element method (BEM) for modeling the electrostatic near-field of a conducting AFM tip in response to an external background field. The tip is represented by a simplified closed, blunt-cone geometry with its surface discretized into panels using increased mesh density near the apex. A modified Green's function is used to calculate interactions between surface panels, while an augmented linear system simultaneously enforces the equipotential boundary condition on the conducting surface and overall charge neutrality to determine the induced surface-charge distribution. We validate the BEM by comparing the resulting near-field decay with that obtained from a three-dimensional quasi-static COMSOL Multiphysics simulation. The BEM accurately reproduces the COMSOL field-decay behavior while requiring orders of magnitude fewer mesh elements and substantially reducing computational cost. These results demonstrate that the BEM provides an accurate and computationally efficient framework for systematically investigating the dependence of THz near-field confinement on AFM tip geometry. This framework can be extended to more complex and experimentally realistic tip geometries, providing a route toward rational tip selection and design for application-specific spatial-resolution requirements.



[9]. [*Kohler's Rule Holds in the Strange Metallic Hubbard Model*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24208 "Kohler's Rule Holds in the Strange Metallic Hubbard Model")

Rebekah M. Jin, Emily Z. Zhang, Jixun K. Ding, Brian Moritz, Thomas P. Devereaux

arXiv:2610.24208v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Unconventional magnetotransport violating Kohler's rule has been reported in many strongly correlated strange metals, but the exact origin of these violations is yet unknown. To investigate this question, we use determinant quantum Monte Carlo to compute the magnetoresistance of the hole-doped Hubbard-Hofstadter model. Despite $T$-linear resistivity exceeding the Mott-Ioffe-Regel limit and the nominal absence of well-defined quasiparticles, we find Kohler's rule unexpectedly holds across a wide range of on-site interaction strengths and hole dopings. In particular, Kohler emerges below a crossover temperature $T^\star\approx t$ identified from the double occupancy and specific heat, coinciding with the onset of local singlet correlations between lattice sites. We thus propose that Kohler scaling in this regime reflects a more general form of many-body quantum coherence. Our results show that strong correlations and strange metallicity do not themselves generically produce anomalous magnetoresistance.



[10]. [*Role of chirality in the polarizer-filter dichotomy in nonmagnetic junctions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24226 "Role of chirality in the polarizer-filter dichotomy in nonmagnetic junctions")

Sergei Urazhdin

arXiv:2610.24226v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Chirality-induced spin selectivity can enable spin generation by nonmagnetic junctions, but its symmetry requirements remain debated. We show that the symmetry of the junction constrains its polarizing behavior: a spin polarizer requires a chiral junction, whereas an achiral junction can act as a spin filter. This symmetry involves the junction as a whole, not only the molecule. Achiral molecules can act as polarizers or filters depending on junction geometry, as confirmed with a minimal tight-binding model. Reciprocity forbids polarization detection by linear two-terminal magnetoresistance; we propose a nonlocal measurement that distinguishes polarizers from filters and establishes whether the junction breaks time-reversal symmetry.



[11]. [*A Chiral Superlattice Route to Spin-Split Topological Antiferromagnetism*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24242 "A Chiral Superlattice Route to Spin-Split Topological Antiferromagnetism")

Thao Dinh, Mengke Liu, Jian-Xiang Qiu, Sougata Mardanya, Suk Hyun Sung, Xuan Hoang Le, Christopher Broyles, Chengfeng Zhu, Qiaozhi Xu, Haotian Chen, Zack Rehfuss, Yu-Fei Liu, Xiaoyu Zeng, Houchen Li, Peng Guo, Jinchen Liu, Tianye Huang, Jingtian Shi, Michael Smith, Joanna M. Blawat, John Singleton, Ross McDonald, Ivan E. Arvizo, Dongtao Cui, Shao-Liang Zheng, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Vineet Kumar Sharma, Sudip Ghorai, Barun Ghosh, Hsin Lin, Ting Yong Lim, Tay-Rong Chang, Arun Bansil, Ivar Martin, Ashvin Vishwanath, Weiwei Xie, Claudia Felser, Jairo Sinova, Anyuan Gao, Mikhail D. Lukin, Jennifer E. Hoffman, Sugata Chowdhury, Qiong Ma, Kai Sun, Ismail El Baggari, Sheng Ran, Talieh S. Ghiasi, Hongkun Park, Philip Kim, Su-Yang Xu

arXiv:2610.24242v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The interplay between chirality and magnetism is a profound topic, spanning particle physics, condensed matter, chemistry and origin of life. In this paper, we study this interplay in the new antiferromagnet (AFM) UOTe, and we report a chiral superlattice route to spin-split topological phenomena from collinear antiferromagnetism. Pristine UOTe is a collinear AFM without Berry curvature or spin-split bands. However, we identify the spontaneous formation of a chiral superlattice in UOTe, which can be viewed as frozen chiral phonons at finite wave vector. When electrons move through the chiral superlattice, their orbital Bloch wavefunction and quantum geometry are modulated by the strong chiral superlattice potential, generating large Berry curvature in the chiral superlattice, which we detect by the nonlinear Hall effect. At 150 K, the chiral superlattice induced Berry curvature couples to the collinear AFM order, generating a giant anomalous Hall angle ~0.14 abruptly near $T_N$ that is among the largest in bulk magnets. Moreover, our spin Hanle precession measurement shows that the chiral superlattice also generates spin-polarized current from the collinear AFM, a long-standing goal in spintronics. We shed light on the supermodulation formation mechanism based on chemical ion size and physical interlayer-intralayer energy competitions, using which we propose a design principle to discover similar bond-mismatch superlattices, providing a chiral superlattice pathway through real space engineering of quantum geometry.



[12]. [*Charge disproportionation and spin ordering in LiV$_2$O$_5$ and NaV$_2$O$_5$: a distributed moment point of view*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24263 "Charge disproportionation and spin ordering in LiV$_2$O$_5$ and NaV$_2$O$_5$: a distributed moment point of view")

Claudio Garcia, Ilteris K. Turan, Jerome Jackson, Swagata Acharya, Mark van Schilfgaarde, Walter R. L. Lambrecht

arXiv:2610.24263v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The effects of charge disproportionation between V$^{4+}$ and V$^{5+}$ on the exchange interactions and related magnetic, electronic and optical properties are studied in LiV$_2$O$_5$ and NaV$_2$O$_5$. The magnetic exchange interactions between local moments on atomic sites are calculated using a linear response approach based on the QS$GW$ band structure. While the induced magnetic moments are small on the vanadyl O$_v$ and bridge O$_b$, their exchange interactions with the V are found to be larger than between V atoms. The N\'eel temperature $T_N$ are calculated within the mean-field (MFA) and random phase (RPA) approximations for two different crystal structures with $P2_1mn$ and $Pmmn$ spacegroups. The critical temperatures are are corrected by a quantum statistical factor of (S+1)/S to obtain reasonable agreement with experiment.
  Band structures, optical dielectric functions calculated using the Bethe Salpeter Equation method and spin waves are also calculated and presented for both crystal structures and both compounds. The spin wave dispersions are calculated from the classical Heisenberg Hamiltonian including magnetic moments on both V and O atoms. Besides the lowest energy weakly interacting spin waves of the two chains of spins in the unit cell, the model predicts additional high energy optical spin waves, which correspond to more complex spin excitations in which the spins on the two V across a bridge, vanadyl O and bridge O precess independently from each other instead of staying locked together according to their collinear alignment in the ground state. These show only weak dispersion and extend up to about 1 eV but we argue that these are an artefact of using the Heisenberg model beyond its validity range and would be suppressed in a more complete theory that allows for coupling of the transverse spin with longitudinal spin and charge excitations.



[13]. [*Tunable anisotropy of high-harmonic generation in ferroelectric NbOI2*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24302 "Tunable anisotropy of high-harmonic generation in ferroelectric NbOI2")

Inho Lee, Christopher Lane, Daniel A. Rehn, Jian-Xin Zhu

arXiv:2610.24302v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: he generation of even-order harmonic spectra from inversion symmetry breaking is well known, however, the tunability of the intensity and angular anisotropy on various harmonics orders remains unclear. Here, we demonstrate the second and fourth-harmonics in the ferroelectric NbOI2 exhibit distinct wavelength-dependence and anisotropies in the near-infrared regime. In particular, as the wavelength of incident light is tuned from 2150 nm to 2450 nm, the second-harmonic remains strongest for excitation along the ferroelectric polar axis, whereas the polarization of the fourth- harmonic changes preferred axis. This effect is found to be driven by the interplay of transition dipole matrix elements and interband excitations between very narrow bands near the Fermi level. We expect the tunable anisotropy of high-harmonics to enable novel optical switching devices.



[14]. [*Encoding Hyperuniformity in Geometric Networks: Structural Signatures and Diffusion Dynamics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24317 "Encoding Hyperuniformity in Geometric Networks: Structural Signatures and Diffusion Dynamics")

Yongyi Wang, Eli Newby, Yang Jiao, Salvatore Torquato, R\'eka Albert

arXiv:2610.24317v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Disordered hyperuniformity is a recently discovered exotic state exhibiting anomalously suppressed density fluctuations at long wavelengths, and its generalization to network systems remains an important open question. Here, we show that, across a wide range of systems, geometric networks retain substantially more long-wavelength density information from the underlying point configurations than Delaunay triangulations. Within geometric networks, hyperuniformity manifests as suppressed vertex-to-vertex fluctuations in a range of network structural observables, including closeness centrality, vertex strength, and eigenvector centrality. These measures not only distinguish hyperuniform networks from non-hyperuniform ones, but also separate near-hyperuniform configurations from hyperuniform systems with strong local disorder, solving a central challenge in network hyperuniformity. We connect these structural signatures to network dynamics by introducing a relaxation-time centrality based on diffusion and demonstrate that hyperuniform geometric networks exhibit more homogeneous relaxation behavior than non-hyperuniform geometric networks. These results reveal how the suppression of large-scale density fluctuations manifests in both network structure and diffusion dynamics.



[15]. [*Maximum-Temperature Theorem for a Nanothermometer*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24340 "Maximum-Temperature Theorem for a Nanothermometer")

Taras Plakhotnik

arXiv:2610.24340v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The steady-state temperature predicted for an ideal point heat source diverges in conventional heat-conduction theory, but no physical thermometer measures a point value. We derive a sharp, shape-independent upper bound on the quasi-steady temperature rise of a sensor with thermometric volume $V_\mathrm{s}$, placed anywhere inside a region enclosed by an isothermal reference surface. The bound requires only the total heat power generated in the region $\dot{Q}$, a lower bound on its thermal conductivity $\kappa_\mathrm{m}$, and the value of $V_\mathrm{s}$. It is otherwise independent of the heat-source distribution, domain shape, and source--sensor distance. For $\dot{Q}=50~\mathrm{pW}$, $\kappa_\mathrm{m} = 0.1~\mathrm{W\,m^{-1}K^{-1}}$, and $V_\mathrm{s}=10^{-22}\mathrm{\,m^3}$, approximately the volume of a $60~\mathrm{nm}$-diameter sphere, the theorem permits a maximum temperature rise of approximately $2~\mathrm{mK}$ above the reference level. The result applies directly to nanodiamonds, upconversion nanoparticles, fluorescent polymer particles, and core-shell quantum dots. It shows that neither source localization nor uncertain source-sensor separation can account for kelvin-scale readings under steady-state heat conduction.



[16]. [*Microphase Separation and Ionic Aggregation in Oppositely Charged End-Functionalized Polymer Blends*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24361 "Microphase Separation and Ionic Aggregation in Oppositely Charged End-Functionalized Polymer Blends")

Chao Duan, Zhen-Gang Wang

arXiv:2610.24361v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We study the phase behavior of binary blends of polymers with oppositely charged termini using an ion-correlation-augmented self-consistent field theory (IC-SCFT). Under the low dielectric conditions typical for organic polymer melts, strong ion correlations drive association of multiple chain ends, leading to emergent multichain architectures that go beyond a simple ion-paired diblock copolymer picture previously used to interpret recent experiments. At moderate to strong polymer segregation, the resulting phase diagram differs considerably from this effective-diblock prediction: ion clustering broadens the lamellar and cylindrical windows while suppressing the network phases, the reverse of the trend for end-charged diblock copolymers. At weak segregation, the two polymers remain miscible even as ion clustering persists, and decreasing chain length drives the clusters themselves through a sphere-to-cylinder-to-lamella shape transition, connecting continuously to the aggregation behavior of classical terminally charged ionomers in the limit when one of the polymer species shrinks to just the charged end. These results reveal that ion clustering -- beyond simple pairwise bonding -- shapes the self-assembly of end-charged polymer blends.



[17]. [*Quantum state tomography through multi-frequency Josephson effect*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24391 "Quantum state tomography through multi-frequency Josephson effect")

Xian-Peng Zhang, Ting-Rui Hu, Zhen-Biao Yang, Rub\'en Seoane Souto, Yugui Yao

arXiv:2610.24391v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Macroscopic quantum effects emerge when the coherent behavior of countless microscopic quantum states manifests collectively at a visible scale, as exemplified by the supercurrent carried by innumerable finite-momentum Cooper pairs. By rigorously analyzing the time evolution of superconducting nanodevices including finite-momentum superconductors and quantum-dot Josephson junctions, we analytically derive the ubiquitous macroscopic supercurrent that originates from quantum interference between distinct many-body eigenstates, which we term as the dynamic interference supercurrent. The dynamic interference supercurrent in a system of two Andreev spin qubits exhibits multi-frequency Josephson effect in the absence of external voltage and is governed by the response of the many-body eigenfunctions ($\vert r\rangle$) to variations in the superconducting phase $\phi$, determined by the $\phi$-space Berry connection $C_{rr'}=\langle r|\partial_\phi|r'\rangle$. The macroscopic dynamic interference supercurrent, an inherent property of the superposition state itself, enables an advanced quantum entanglement measurement of the Andreev spin qubits. These findings open new avenues for investigating superconducting nanodevice properties from a wave-function level, with substantial implications for advancing quantum science and technology in solid-state superconducting devices, such as quantum computer, communication, memory, and sensor.



[18]. [*Second Chern Number Protected Gapless Vortex Modes in Superconducting Weyl Semimetals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24401 "Second Chern Number Protected Gapless Vortex Modes in Superconducting Weyl Semimetals")

Zhicheng Zhang, Jiangping Hu

arXiv:2610.24401v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We have studied the vortex bound states of superconducting linear and quadratic Weyl semimetals with time reversal symmetry. For vortex with vorticity $n$ (vortex line along $z$ direction) and Weyl semimetals with Weyl point chirality $\chi$, there are $\vert n\chi \vert$ gapless vortex modes around the location of each Weyl point in superconducting Weyl semimetals. We show that the appearance of $\vert n\chi \vert$ gapless vortex modes originates from the imbalanced pumping of flux bound states between Chern insulators obtained at $k_{z}$ and $-k_{z}$ in Weyl semimetals. Such a physical picture is verified by numerical calculations on lattice models. Moreover, the gapless modes around each Weyl point can be characterized by the second Chern number defined in a synthetic four-dimensional space based on the effective BdG Hamiltonian. Finally, these gapless vortex modes are found to be robust against rotational symmetry-breaking terms.



[19]. [*Atomistic prediction of magnetic behaviour across chemistry, structure and temperature*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24407 "Atomistic prediction of magnetic behaviour across chemistry, structure and temperature")

Ruosong Shi, Chengbing Chen, Bicheng Liu, Daye Zheng, Duo Zhang, Zhengtao Huang, Teng Yang, Zefeng Cai, Xingliang Peng, Xinzijian Liu, Shenshen Yan, Xueqing Zhao, Jianbo Wang, Bin Liu, Han Wang, Ke Wang, Liuliu Han, Ben Xu

arXiv:2610.24407v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Controlling magnetic phases and properties through composition and external conditions is central to the development of magnetic materials. Atomistic foundation models offer a route to predicting such behaviour across diverse materials, but magnetic materials remain challenging because their atomic structure and local moments are strongly coupled, while temperature and disorder drive magnetic moments into non-collinear states with evolving orientations and magnitudes. Broadly pretrained atomistic models still sample only limited magnetic diversity, while finite-temperature descriptions of coupled lattice and magnetic fluctuations remain largely material-specific. Here we introduce MagPT, pretrained across 48 elements on 912,492 spin-lattice configurations spanning structural, orientational and longitudinal magnetic fluctuations over 0 $\sim$ 1,000 K and 0 $\sim$ 10 GPa. By treating species, atomic positions and vector moments together, MagPT distinguishes magnetic states that share the same atomic structure but differ in spin configuration and energy. It captures magnetic behaviour across composition, temperature and length scales, from Slater-Pauling-type composition-magnetism relations in transition-metal alloys and finite-temperature phase evolution in elemental ferromagnets and magnetic shape-memory Heusler alloys, to emergent long-wavelength non-collinear spin textures in chiral magnets and local chemistry-magnetism coupling in high-entropy alloys. MagPT moves atomistic studies of magnetic materials beyond material-specific approaches, establishing a general framework for exploring and designing magnetic matter across diverse chemistries and conditions.



[20]. [*Calibrated shape-only recovery of membrane viscosities*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24410 "Calibrated shape-only recovery of membrane viscosities")

Julian Olszewski, Zijian Liu, Jie Xu

arXiv:2610.24410v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Nearby relaxation rates make two membrane viscosities difficult to distinguish from a shape transient. We show how calibrated preparation removes this loss of local sensitivity in a linear Kelvin-Voigt sphere with finite area elasticity and intrinsic membrane dissipation. Two independent releases, with a known signed amplitude ratio and calibrated relative gain and timing, yield normal-displacement records whose fixed linear combination separates the shear and dilatational rates. Their common amplitude remains unknown. For fixed sampling, positive-definite weights, bounded positive rates and nonzero excitation, the amplitude-projected sensitivity has a positive lower bound even at rate coincidence. Without relative calibration, the paired sensitivity decreases linearly with the rate gap; the single tangential-release record decreases quadratically. The calibrated bound is compatible with fixed finite initial energy. A positive local bound also persists with independent ratio calibration of fixed positive precision under a Gaussian error model. Supplied elastic coefficients convert the recovered rates into the two surface viscosities. An unbounded spherical Stokes extension quantifies how bulk dissipation changes the modal patterns and provides a sufficient full-Jacobian criterion for retaining local material sensitivity.

Liquids in porous silicon, most often water, can flow as a continuum through pores a few nanometers wide, strain the skeleton through capillary and electrosorption stresses, shift structural color, switch transparency, and modulate or generate electrical signals. We describe three directions to combine and extend these effects and overcome their limitations. Pore hierarchy resolves the classical trade-off between interface area and transport speed by combining large and small pores and thereby allows functions to be scaled from thin planar films to three-dimensional components. Surface modification becomes a design variable that tunes the interaction between water and the pore wall. It can be set locally and, with switchable coatings, on demand. Finally, pores only a few molecules wide alter the properties of water itself. In the absence of a bulk-like volume, water can become dielectrically anisotropic and highly conductive. Altogether, these steps turn porous silicon from a responsive material into an adaptive, water-driven material.

Abstract: Pretrained diffusion models provide powerful priors for generating new materials, including inorganic crystals, but generating useful materials requires satisfying specific chemical and physical properties, from mechanical stiffness to bandgaps. The resulting analysis shows that the point contact tip-film-substrate problem is governed by a single dimensionless invariant, $\Omega = R_{ss}\kappa_{\mathrm{sub}}t$, and two control parameters: the confinement ratio $t/b$ and the thermal contrast $\kappa_{\mathrm{sub}}/\kappa_{\mathrm{film}}$. From the invariant, we derive an amplification factor, set only by the contact geometry and the interfaces, that converts the uncertainty of a measurement into an uncertainty in the extracted conductivity. Two explicitly constructed ensembles with identical mean and complete two-point correlation function yield $g^{(2)}=3/4$ and $9/8$, placing their counting variances on opposite sides of the Poisson value. [67]. [*Self-Trapping and Liouvillian Spectral Transitions in a Dissipative Bose-Hubbard Dimer*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25222 "Self-Trapping and Liouvillian Spectral Transitions in a Dissipative Bose-Hubbard Dimer")

[71]. Grain boundaries of the (Hf,Ta,Ti,Nb,Zr)B$_2$ preferentially include Nb and Ta. This lowers the grain boundary energy and strengthens the boundaries. This consists of the lower boundary energy and changes Fermi level bonding in NbB$_2$ and TaB$_2$.

[76]. [81]. [*Chiral symmetry and the orbital susceptibility of disordered honeycomb flakes*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25555 "Chiral symmetry and the orbital susceptibility of disordered honeycomb flakes")

Abstract: The orbital susceptibility of a small disordered grain is dominated by rare samples in which an occupied and an empty level nearly touch. In a neutral honeycomb flake, chiral (sublattice) symmetry removes the second condition: It places the chemical potential in the middle of the smallest gap, in every disorder realization. Here we show that the mean orbital susceptibility then grows as $\ln(1/T)$ at low temperature $T$ if disorder can close this gap while a magnetic field still mixes the two levels, and that the hopping geometry decides whether it can: Positive nearest-neighbor hopping on a compact hexagonal flake keeps the gap open, third-neighbor hopping allows it to close. In a chiral random-matrix model with $n$ level pairs the coefficient of the logarithm is $C_n=\sqrt{2/n}\,\Gamma[(n+1)/2]/\Gamma(n/2)$, close to unity, and a detuned chemical potential cuts the logarithm off.

Abstract: Annually about one billion tires reach the end of life. Rubber from waste tires can be ground into particles and incorporated into a matrix. The principle of flaw-tolerant recycling may aid the development of recycling waste tires into new tires.

Abstract: We illustrate the field-driven anomalous Hall engineering via the momentum-space motion of Weyl nodes using pyrochlore iridates under a [111] magnetic field as an example. Tracking the position of Weyl nodes during the spin rotation, we demonstrate macroscopic anomalous Hall conductivity variations are fundamentally driven by Weyl node motions by comparing the full multi-band Kubo formula with the geometric Weyl node separation. The discrepancy occurs only near a critical spin rotation angle, where several Weyl nodes come into close proximity. We reveal that the evolution of anomalous Hall conductivity under the magnetic field agrees with reported experiments in an order-of-magnitude and non-monotonicity. These results elucidate the field-driven dynamics of momentum-space monopoles and provide a framework for understanding transport in strongly correlated topological materials.



[85]. [*Autonomous adaptive spectral analysis with an LLM agent for self-driving laboratories*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25619 "Autonomous adaptive spectral analysis with an LLM agent for self-driving laboratories")

Yuki K. Wakabayashi, Yui Ogawa

arXiv:2610.25619v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Self-driving laboratories accelerate materials exploration through autonomous closed-loop integration of synthesis, characterization, and next-experiment selection. However, characterization does not always directly provide the target property; measured spectra or images often require automated analysis to extract quantitative descriptors based on physically motivated assumptions and predefined criteria. Such procedures can fail for measurements outside these criteria, requiring a decision on whether a reliable value can be recovered or no numerical value should be reported. Here, we implemented an autonomous adaptive spectral-analysis agent using a large language model (LLM) to supervise a deterministic numerical-analysis routine. For each spectrum, the agent executes the predefined analysis script and assesses the result using the measured spectrum and fitting diagnostics. Valid results are accepted unchanged, recoverable failures are reanalyzed through bounded adjustments of predefined fitting parameters, and NaN (not a number) is returned when no scientifically defensible value can be obtained. We evaluated the workflow on optical transmittance spectra from 203 epitaxial beta-Ga2O3 films grown during a previously reported self-driving thin-film growth campaign. The predefined optical analysis script returned Urbach energy, an optical-quality metric, for 183 spectra and failed for 20. The agent retained those 183 values and evaluated 20 exception cases, recovering 16 through bounded parameter adjustments and returning NaN for 4 when the spectral evidence did not support a reliable Urbach tail. The audit records captured spectral features, parameter changes, and rationale for each decision. These results demonstrate autonomous scientific exception handling by an LLM agent while leaving quantitative property extraction to deterministic numerical analysis.



[86]. [*Categorical classification of non-Fermi liquids in 2+1 dimensions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25713 "Categorical classification of non-Fermi liquids in 2+1 dimensions")

Shubham Kukreja, Dawson M. Willerton, Sung-Sik Lee

arXiv:2610.25713v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: A defining feature of non-Fermi liquids is a lack of the quasiparticle peak on the Fermi surface in the spectral function, which is usually realized by coupling a finite density of fermions with critical collective fluctuations. However, these features of the two- and three-point vertex functions are qualitatively the same across many non-Fermi liquids, which has made it difficult to classify them beyond the definition by negation. We point out that the four-point function in the pairing channel contains rich information that positively differentiates distinct types of non-Fermi liquids. Using the generalized notion of fixed points that captures scale-dependent universal pairing interaction, we provide a non-perturbative classification of 2+1-dimensional non-Fermi liquids. In the large Fermi momentum limit with fixed dimensionless couplings, metals coupled with long-wavelength critical fluctuations are classified into seven superuniversality classes. Each of them exhibits a unique phase diagram in the space of temperature and bare four-fermion coupling. The present work generalizes the earlier perturbative classification to strongly interacting non-Fermi liquids by leveraging kinematic constraints.



[87]. [*Crystal Structure Based Graph Neural Network And LLM Multi-Modal Approach for Material Magnetic Ordering Prediction*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25775 "Crystal Structure Based Graph Neural Network And LLM Multi-Modal Approach for Material Magnetic Ordering Prediction")

Adam Zheng, April Zheng, Sheng Ran

arXiv:2610.25775v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: AI/ML models are useful for the prediction of materials' properties. Recently, there has been great progress in developing Neural Network models to predict the properties of solids, molecules, and crystals, avoiding expensive and time-consuming laboratory testing. This study presents a novel AI/ML-based multi-modal approach to predict material magnetic ordering using material crystal structure information as input to a Graph Neural Network model (GNN) and semantic text descriptions of the material's properties and structure as input to a Large Language Model (LLM), before performing information fusion of the GNN and LLM outputs to predict material magnetic ordering in four classes (Non-magnetic, Ferromagnetic, Antiferromagnetic, Ferrimagnetic). The model is trained, validated, and tested on a dataset which is a combination of Materials Project dataset, Magndata dataset, and a set of uranium compounds with magnetic ordering determined in laboratory testing, with an 80/10/10 (training/validation/testing) data split. The proposed GNN and LLM multi-modal approach achieves better overall accuracy than both the standalone GNN and LLM approaches with three fusion strategies.



[88]. [*Effects of Nonmagnetic Impurities on Nonequilibrium Phase Transitions in Microwave-Driven Superconductors*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25859 "Effects of Nonmagnetic Impurities on Nonequilibrium Phase Transitions in Microwave-Driven Superconductors")

arXiv:2610.25859v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate the nonequilibrium steady states of a microwave-driven BCS superconductor in the presence of nonmagnetic impurity scattering and energy relaxation. Using the Floquet-Nambu-Keldysh Green's function method together with the self-consistent Born approximation, we self-consistently determine the superconducting order parameter in the microwave-driven steady state. We find that the nonequilibrium phase diagram depends strongly on the impurity-scattering strength. When impurity scattering is absent or weak, the steady-state order parameter is single-valued at each temperature and drive frequency. At intermediate scattering strengths, we find bistability between microwave-enhanced and microwave-suppressed superconducting states. As the impurity-scattering strength increases further, we instead find bistability between the superconducting and normal states. This evolution reflects competition between two effects of microwave driving: Eliashberg-type enhancement through the redistribution of thermally excited quasiparticles and suppression through pair-breaking-induced quasiparticle generation. In contrast to the robustness of equilibrium $s$-wave superconductivity against nonmagnetic impurity scattering, our results demonstrate that nonmagnetic impurity scattering plays a central role in determining the character of nonequilibrium superconducting phase transitions under microwave driving.



[89]. [*Phase transitions of the antiferromagnetic $J_1$-$J_2$-$J_3$ Ising model on the cubic lattice*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25884 "Phase transitions of the antiferromagnetic $J_1$-$J_2$-$J_3$ Ising model on the cubic lattice")

arXiv:2610.25884v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Motivated by the conflicting findings in prior studies regarding the nature of the phase transition for the three-dimensional antiferromagnetic $J_1$-$J_2$-$J_3$ Ising model on the cubic lattice with $J_1 = J_2 = J_3 = 1.0$, in this study we conduct a detailed investigation of this model using both Monte Carlo simulations and neural networks (NN). Here $J_1$ and $J_2$ are the intralayer nearest-neighbor and next-nearest-neighbor couplings, respectively, and $J_3$ is the nearest-neighbor interlayer interaction. By investigating the data obtained from much larger lattices than those used in previous studies, we find that the phase transition for $J_1 = J_2 = J_3 = 1.0$ is second order without any ambiguity. Hence we bring a resolution to this debate. Apart from $J_1 = J_2 = J_3 = 1.0$, we study the critical phenomenon associated with $J_2/J_1 = 4/7$ and $J_3 = J_1 = 1.0$ as well. In addition, by employing a dedicated training and testing strategy, our NN computation not only is much more efficient than those utilizing the standard procedures commonly used in the literature, but also requires only a per-mille fraction of the storage capacity needed by a conventional NN calculation when a large linear system size $L$ is considered. The method employed here provides an extremely resource-efficient route for applying NN techniques to study the critical behaviors of higher-dimensional systems.



[90]. [*A Stress-Induced Tetragonal Distortion in K2OsCl6 under Pressure*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25913 "A Stress-Induced Tetragonal Distortion in K2OsCl6 under Pressure")

Neha Bura, Peije Zhang, Ganesh Bera, Catalin Popescu, Caleb J. Bennett, Brendan J. Kennedy, Daniel Errandonea

arXiv:2610.25913v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The structural behaviour of cubic (Fm-3m) K2OsCl6 under compression was investigated to 35.4 GPa using angle-dispersive synchrotron powder X-ray diffraction. Upon increasing pressure, K2OsCl6 undergoes a sequence of structural transformations from the ambient-pressure cubic phase to a tetragonal (P4/mnc) phase above 9.5(3) GPa, followed by a transition to a monoclinic (P21/n) phase beginning at approximately 14.2 GPa. [91]. [*Ferroelastic domain wall driven glassy dynamics and photoresponse in a SrTiO$_3$ (111) two-dimensional electron gas*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25943 "Ferroelastic domain wall driven glassy dynamics and photoresponse in a SrTiO$_3$ (111) two-dimensional electron gas")

arXiv:2610.25943v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The (111) orientation of SrTiO$_3$ (STO)-based heterostructures has emerged as a versatile platform for two-dimensional electron gases (2DEGs), yet the influence of STO's polar ferroelastic domain-wall (DW) network on charge transport remains largely unexplored. Here we study AlO$_x$/STO (111) interfaces hosting 2DEGs of different carrier densities. In the lower-density 2DEG, low-temperature transport exhibits thermal hysteresis, slow resistance relaxation, cooling-rate dependence, and memory effects, signaling glasslike electron dynamics. The relaxation is strongly non-monotonic in temperature, increasing below $\sim$80 K, peaking near 25 K, and vanishing below $\sim$12 K, tracking the established evolution of the ferroelastic DW state of bulk STO rather than any electronic energy scale, which identifies the DW network as its origin. These signatures are absent in a second, more metallic interface with much higher carrier density, demonstrating that the electron-DW coupling can be tuned. We further show that polar DWs govern the photoresponse: the persistent photoconductivity rises sharply below $\sim$80 K, coinciding with the onset of DW polarity. Together, these results establish polar ferroelastic DWs as a carrier-density-tunable electronic degree of freedom in STO (111)-based 2DEGs, opening routes to manipulate 2DEG properties through topological defects.



[92]. [*Effect of chirality on active elastomers and solids in three dimensions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25945 "Effect of chirality on active elastomers and solids in three dimensions")

S. J. Kole, Gareth P. Alexander, Ananyo Maitra, Sriram Ramaswamy

arXiv:2610.25945v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We examine the effect of chiral activity on nematic and polar uniaxial elastomers and solids by constructing the general hydrodynamic equations for these phases. We start with the elastomer, which is not only invariant under rigid rotations but also under an independent rotation of the uniaxial direction, and show that this higher symmetry strongly constrains the effects of chiral activity. We then break the elastomeric symmetry by adding a symmetry-breaking term to the free energy but still retain invariance under rigid rotations. The resulting state has the symmetry of a uniaxial solid, and we show that in a fixed background medium chiral activity generically destabilises it. The force density responsible for this {{instability}} vanishes as the field that breaks the elastomeric symmetry goes to zero. Further, the presence of the symmetry-breaking field also allows for an additional odd elastic force density that generically destabilises momentum-conserved chiral and uniaxial solids in the inertial regime.



[93]. [*Chirality destroys oriented active solids*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25953 "Chirality destroys oriented active solids")

S. J. Kole, Gareth P. Alexander, Ananyo Maitra, Sriram Ramaswamy

arXiv:2610.25953v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We construct a hydrodynamic theory of the effect of chiral and active force densities on three-dimensional uniaxial solids. We further show this active instability also affects isotropic chiral solids in an anisotropic medium, a situation that is relevant for cell clusters moving through the extracellular matrix. Interestingly, the instability is eliminated in a higher-symmetry solid, known as the polar elastomer; indeed, we show that chirality does not affect the leading-order mechanics of such materials. We further demonstrate that momentum-conserving polar active solids have two chiral odd elasticities in contrast to the one discussed for two-dimensional solids. [94]. [*Interfacial origin of magnetic compensation and topological-like Hall effect in crystalline lutetium iron garnet thin films.*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25965 "Interfacial origin of magnetic compensation and topological-like Hall effect in crystalline lutetium iron garnet thin films.")

Satakshi Pandey (CEA), Anne Forget (CEA), Sarah Clement (CEMES), Christophe Gatel (CEMES), B{\'e}n{\'e}dicte Warot- Fonrose (CEMES), Gr{\'e}goire de Loubens (CEA), Jean-Baptiste Moussy (CEA)

Abstract: Ferrimagnetic oxides such as rare-earth iron garnets (REIG) have emerged as essential components of spin-based devices owing to their high Curie temperatures, low Gilbert damping, and excellent insulating properties. Recently, REIGs have attracted immense interest for hosting robust chiral spin textures that can be potential information carriers in next-generation spintronic devices. We further probe the anomalous Hall effect (AHE) in LuIG layers with an adjacent platinum ultrathin film. In addition to the magnetic compensation point, the AHE loops show pronounced nonmonotonic features, often attributed to the spin Hall topological-like Hall effect (SH-THE) and non-collinear spin textures arising from the Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction (DMI). LuIG thickness-dependent AHE measurements reveal that the composition variation due to Gd diffusion breaks the inversion symmetry and could contribute to DMI. These findings shed light on fundamental understanding and engineering of the different interfaces of magnetic garnets for advanced spintronic device applications.



[95]. [*Methods of density functional theory in one dimension*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26012 "Methods of density functional theory in one dimension")

Thomas E. Baker, Kasey Chen

arXiv:2610.26012v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Density functional theory is formally justified by an existence proof. As such, the need for computational methods to study the theory is a useful route to understanding the full theory. In this paper, we discuss the numerical strategies to efficiently evaluate computations for Kohn-Sham systems in one dimension and also methods for determining exact properties, including time-dependence. An accompanying library is also available for computing one-dimensional density functional theory and publicly available. The expressions used to implement the density functional in one-dimension are surveyed. An alternative justification for a gradient-based technique is also shown that can be applied to time-dependent evolution of the exact Kohn-Sham potential.



[96]. [*Phase engineering of odd parity state in a quantum dot Josephson junction*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26015 "Phase engineering of odd parity state in a quantum dot Josephson junction")

Shohei Kobayashi, Tyler Lindemann, Sergei Gronin, Geoffrey Gardner, Peter Stano, Daniel Loss, Michael Manfra, Seigo Tarucha, Sadashige Matsuo

arXiv:2610.26015v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Controlling fermion parity in quantum-dot Josephson junctions (QDJJs) enables access to novel superconducting physics. While parity transitions are usually tuned electrically, we study phase-induced transitions in a strongly coupled QDJJ using the superconducting phase difference across the dot terminals, denoted by $\phi$. We find that this phase allows control over the ground state parity, with parity transitions observed as the Andreev bound states (ABSs) energies crossing zero. These crossings occur near $\phi = \pi$, and their behavior is in good correspondence to a simple model considering a single-level quantum dot proximitized by a superconductor. These results place phase-controlled QDJJ arrays as a promising platform for exploring exotic phenomena such as Majorana zero modes.



[97]. [*Gini coefficient of scientists and universities*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26053 "Gini coefficient of scientists and universities")

arXiv:2610.26053v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Using the public database of Google Scholar article citations of scientists and universities we apply the algorithm for constructing Lorenz curves to the resulting citation distributions. Using Lorenz and Pareto curves, broadly used for economic analyses of wealth inequality across countries, we show that this approach gives a useful insight of citation distributions of world-leading scientists characterized by the Gini coefficient $G$ obtained from the Lorenz curves. For articles of 15 Nobel laureates (including A. Einstein and R. Feynman) we find the average $G=0.81$ with 50% of articles capturing only 2.7% of all citations and the top 10% (1%) of articles capturing 71% (30%) of all citations. Furthmore, we determine for world-leading universities the Gini coefficient via the citations of affiliated scientists. We show that about 65 percent of all citations belonging to a university (e.g. Princeton, Cambridge) are generated only by 10 percent of their university scientists while 50% of scientists produce only 3% of all university citations. The citation distributions are well described by the concept of Rayleigh-Jeans thermalization and condensation with similarities traced to the world wealth inequality.



[98]. [*Magnetoelectric Bulk Photovoltaic Effect in Centrosymmetric Solids*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26057 "Magnetoelectric Bulk Photovoltaic Effect in Centrosymmetric Solids")

Longjun Xiang, Fuming Xu, Lei Zhang, Jian Wang

arXiv:2610.26057v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: It is commonly believed that a bulk photovoltaic current requires broken spatial inversion symmetry. However, in this work, we demonstrate that dc photocurrents can arise in centrosymmetric solids from the bilinear coupling between the electric and magnetic components of light. This magnetoelectric bulk photovoltaic effect (MBPE) has a quantum-geometric origin in the dipole moments of the Zeeman quantum metric and Zeeman Berry curvature. We identify four distinct MBPE mechanisms, including time-reversal-even and time-reversal-odd injection-like and shift-like photocurrents driven by linearly or circularly polarized light. Our symmetry analysis shows that the MBPE is allowed in broad classes of two-dimensional centrosymmetric nonmagnetic and magnetic materials under normally incident light. As representative examples, we predict sizable MBPEs in centrosymmetric monolayer Pt$_2$HgSe$_3$ and altermagnetic RuF$_4$, with current densities on the order of $1~\mathrm{mA/m}$. Notably, the injection-like photocurrent in RuF$_4$ reverses sign upon switching either the light helicity or the N\'eel vector. Our results extend the bulk photovoltaic effect to centrosymmetric solids and open new opportunities for electrical readout of altermagnetic order.



[99]. [*evaluating local viscoelastic properties of artery-mimicking phantoms using high-resolution flexural wavenumber analysis*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26075 "evaluating local viscoelastic properties of artery-mimicking phantoms using high-resolution flexural wavenumber analysis")

arXiv:2610.26075v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The measurement of guided wave propagation is an effective approach for evaluating arterial wall stiffness. A more comprehensive characterization of viscoelastic material properties could yield more informative biomarkers; however, this additionally requires estimation of wave attenuation. Furthermore, spatially local estimation is necessary for localized pathologies, such as atherosclerotic plaques. To this end, we propose an original two-stage high-resolution method to recover local viscoelastic properties from guided waves in artery-like soft tubes. First, the local frequency-wavenumber dispersion relation including losses is estimated using a high-resolution complex wavenumber identification method. Second, assuming a fractional Kelvin-Voigt rheological model for the tube wall, we estimate the viscoelastic material parameters by fitting the dispersion relation computed with a spectral element method to this estimate. We experimentally validate the proposed approach on flexural wave propagation along water-filled soft tubes, measured with a high-speed camera coupled with digital image correlation. Owing to the high resolution of the method, the axial modulus variation of a graded phantom is recovered. Finally, a Cram\'er-Rao analysis indicates that the complex wavenumber estimator operates near its theoretical limit, and reveals why local attenuation is intrinsically difficult to recover.



[100]. [*A project of the thermal triple axis spectrometer at the PIK reactor*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26076 "A project of the thermal triple axis spectrometer at the PIK reactor")

Maria Iuzviuk, Nikita Kovalenko, Vladislav Tolichev, Aleksandr Ovsianikov, Oleg Usmanov, Anna Matveeva, Igor Zobkalo, Vladislav Tarnavich

arXiv:2610.26076v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: A project of the novel thermal triple axis spectrometer IN1 at the PIK reactor is presented. Monte Carlo simulations were used for optimization its main parameters. The optimization of the instrument gives an estimated neutron flux as high as 109 n/cm2/s at the sample position at neutron energy about 36 meV at the designed 100 MW power. The modelling of neutron stages like monochromators, analyzers, virtual source, as well as instrument shielding was fulfilled to obtain the best performance of the spectrometer.



[101]. [*Twitching fiberboids in the collective : A table-top, one-dimensional, active particle gas*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26087 "Twitching fiberboids in the collective : A table-top, one-dimensional, active particle gas")

Alex Arnhold, Falko Ziebert, Igor M. Kuli\'{c}

arXiv:2610.26087v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Fiberboids are filaments that self-propel by rolling along a surface due to a flux normal to that surface. In the last few years, fibers using several materials and driving mechanisms have been developed that are able to roll persistently. Here we show using thermally driven nylon fibers on a slightly curved glass surface, that fiberboids also come in another state, that we call ``twitching'': the fiber rolls for a short time and then switches direction, implementing on large scales a diffusion-like motion. We rationalize the occurrence of this effect and experimentally quantify this new dynamic state. We then analyze ensembles of twitching fiberboids and by confining the fibers inside a circular arena to allow them to roll on a single axis only, we implement a simple, table-top realization of a one-dimensional non-equilibrium active gas. We study experimentally active cluster formation, their non-equilibrium dynamics and we rationalize the observations by a simple lattice model. This model takes into account a Viscek-like alignment of the velocity directions of the individual particles within clusters to yield good qualitative agreement with the experiments. Overall our work opens the path to study collectives of self-rolling fibers as an interesting new class of active matter.



[102]. [*Generalised Fluctuating Dynamic Density-Functional Theory: Derivation and Applications*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26129 "Generalised Fluctuating Dynamic Density-Functional Theory: Derivation and Applications")

Toby Kay, Serafim Kalliadasis

arXiv:2610.26129v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Classical density-functional theory is a powerful theoretical framework for studying the equilibrium density distribution of complex many-body systems. The core of the framework is to bridge microscopic and macroscopic descriptions of fluids through a variational principle that minimises the system's free energy. For dynamical settings, dynamic DFT (DDFT) extends the equilibrium framework to capture the time evolution of an order parameter, typically density. However, DDFT is often insufficient for mesoscopic systems far from equilibrium where fluctuations become significant, necessitating a stochastic extension: fluctuating DDFT. Here we derive a generalised FDDFT from first principles starting from the microscopic Hamiltonian dynamics using coarse-graining techniques, including Zwanzig's projection operator method. The resulting FDDFT applies to arbitrary observable fields, where the dynamics are driven by a thermodynamic force expressed in terms of an entropy functional. The dynamics consist of reversible, dissipative, and stochastic contributions, each directly linked to the underlying microscopic dynamics via the Liouville operator, Green-Kubo relations, and the fluctuation-dissipation theorem. To highlight the difference in the level of description between DDFT and FDDFT, we demonstrate how further coarse-graining via averaging leads to a generalised DDFT, valid at the macroscale. To showcase the effectiveness and utility of the generalised FDDFT, we apply it to two prototypical fluid systems: colloidal and simple viscous fluids. In the former case, we utilise the new formalism to re-derive the recently found colloidal FDDFT. In the latter case, we rigorously recover the non-isothermal extension of the Landau-Lifshitz fluctuating Navier-Stokes equations, providing fundamental derivation of this well-known phenomenological model, enabling to clarify its underlying principles.



[103]. [*Evidence of valence bond solid and quantum paramagnetism in the $J_1$--$J_2$--$J_3$ Heisenberg model from pseudofermion functional renormalization group*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26135 "Evidence of valence bond solid and quantum paramagnetism in the $J_1$--$J_2$--$J_3$ Heisenberg model from pseudofermion functional renormalization group")

arXiv:2610.26135v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Motivated by recent tensor network studies reporting a quantum spin liquid (QSL) within the quantum paramagnetic (QP) regime, we investigate numerically the zero-temperature phase diagram of the $J_1$--$J_2$--$J_3$ Heisenberg model on the square lattice using the pseudofermion functional renormalization group (pf-fRG). Thanks to improved frequency resolution and larger lattice truncations, we are able to explore a significantly larger parameter space than previous pf-fRG work to map out the phase boundary of an extended QP region. We further resolve possible bond order within the QP regime by measuring the dimer response to small deformations in $J_1$, i.e. bond anisotropy that breaks the point-group symmetry of the Hamiltonian. The functional renormalization group flow of the dimer susceptibility suggests that the QP window splits into two: a region where it grows continuously down to the lowest energy scale to signal valence bond solid (VBS) order, and another region where it reaches a peak at a finite scale and is subsequently suppressed to indicate a QSL.



[104]. [*Motility-Induced Phase Separation of Run-and-Tumble Particles under Local Stochastic Resetting*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26149 "Motility-Induced Phase Separation of Run-and-Tumble Particles under Local Stochastic Resetting")

arXiv:2610.26149v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate the non-equilibrium stationary states of a one-dimensional active gas composed of run-and-tumble particles (RTPs) undergoing motility-induced phase separation (MIPS) and subjected to local stochastic resetting at the origin. Unlike global resetting, local resetting maintains a mass-injection flux at the origin while preserving large-scale spatial self-organization. We demonstrate that above a critical resetting rate $r_c$, the active system undergoes a transition toward a stationary two-phase state featuring a dense liquid droplet centered at the origin coexisting with a surrounding gas phase. Using kinetic evolution equations, we derive the exact steady-state density profile expressed in terms of the Lambert function. The size of the droplet has a non-monotonic dependence on the resetting rate $r$, and attains its maximum. Furthermore, we analyze the dynamical stability of the supersaturated gas phase and show that a nucleated droplet relaxes exponentially toward its steady-state size with a relaxation timescale of $1/r$. We extend our framework to model a finite resetting velocity, proving that the stationary droplet size is invariant with respect to the resetting velocity.



[105]. [*Superstability of micrometer viscous jets surrounded by weakly-elastic viscous shells*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26150 "Superstability of micrometer viscous jets surrounded by weakly-elastic viscous shells")

A. Rubio-Gonz\'alez, D. Fern\'andez-Mart\'inez, M. Vakili, J. M. Montanero

arXiv:2610.26150v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Our experiments demonstrate the strong stabilizing effect of a viscoelastic shell on flow-focused viscous jets. The results suggest that stabilization occurs when the polymer viscosity, $\eta_p$, exceeds a critical threshold, provided the extensional relaxation time, $\lambda$, is large enough to allow the polymer molecules to undergo the coil-stretch transition. Under these conditions, substantial extensional viscoelastic stresses develop as the liquid accelerates. Since both $\eta_p$ and $\lambda$ increase with shell viscosity, the polymer concentration required to stabilize the viscous jet decreases accordingly. Remarkably, moderately to highly viscous jets can be stabilized with very small polymer concentrations, for which no beads-on-a-string (BOAS) instability is observed.



[106]. [*Nanowire-Assisted Epitaxial Lateral Overgrowth of Planar GaN(0001) on Diamond(100)*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26158 "Nanowire-Assisted Epitaxial Lateral Overgrowth of Planar GaN(0001) on Diamond(100)")

Brijmohan Paramanik, Mayur Shinde, Subhranshu Sekhar Sahu, Soumyadip Chatterjee, Prajwal Pandey, Anshul Jain, Himanjal Bhattacharyya, Alpesh Donga, Apurba Laha

arXiv:2610.26158v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: GaN-on-diamond heterostructures are promising for high-power and high-frequency electronics, however, direct epi- taxial growth on diamond (100) remains challenging due to severe lattice and thermal mismatches, which induce high threading dislocation densities. To overcome this challenge, we have used Plasma Assisted Molecular Beam Epitaxy (PAMBE) to grow wurtzite GaN layers on diamond(100) substrates using nanowire-assisted epitaxial lateral overgrowth (ELOG). Two nanowire templates (NW100, NW600) were benchmarked against a nanowire-free reference (NW000). High-resolution XRD, {\phi} -scans, (0002) pole figures, and reciprocal space mapping show that NW100 attains the lowest dislocation density (5.51 x 10-4 nm-2) and microstrain (2.736 x 10-3), the narrowest (0002) reflection, and a c-axis lattice parameter (5.1821 {\AA}) within 0.06% of the bulk value; NW000, in contrast, exhibits a contracted c-axis (5.1633 {\AA}) and elevated dislocation density (6.26 x 10-4 nm-2), consistent with residual tensile stress. Cross-sectional TEM con- firms the intended diamond/AlN/nanowire/ELOG/planar-GaN layer sequence and shows threading dislocations bending into near-lateral trajectories, directly evidencing defect filtering by the nanowire template. Together, these results estab- lish nanowire-assisted ELOG as an effective strategy to suppress dislocation propagation and recover near-bulk lattice parameters in GaN-on-diamond heteroepitaxy, providing a structurally well-defined platform for device integration.



[107]. [*Bicircular light tunable topological phases and nonlinear Hall responses in odd-parity magnets*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26163 "Bicircular light tunable topological phases and nonlinear Hall responses in odd-parity magnets")

Md Afsar Reja, Awadhesh Narayan

arXiv:2610.26163v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Altermagnets are an emerging class of magnetic materials that exhibit characteristic features of both ferromagnets and antiferromagnets. Odd-wave magnets, where the momentum-dependent spin splitting follows an odd-parity form factor, provide a distinct platform for exploring unconventional magnetic and topological phenomena. In this work, we demonstrate light-induced topological phase transitions in odd-parity magnets driven by bicircular light. We show that a cascade of topological phase transitions occur as the relative amplitude ratio of the two beams is varied. These are accompanied by sharp changes of the quantum metric. We demonstrate that bicircular light unlocks nonlinear Hall responses arising from Berry curvature and quantum metric in irradiated odd-wave magnets, and formulate the underlying selection rules. Our results show that both the topology and the nonlinear responses in odd-parity magnets can be directly controlled by a bicircular drive.



[108]. [*Time-Reversal Symmetry Breaking in the Centrosymmetric Nonsymmorphic Superconductor RuB2*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26264 "Time-Reversal Symmetry Breaking in the Centrosymmetric Nonsymmorphic Superconductor RuB2")

Ritu Gupta,  Reena, Dibyendu Samanta, Jaskaran Singh, Thomas J. Hicken, Hubertus Luetkens, Jonas Krieger, Sudeep Kumar Ghosh, Yogesh Singh

arXiv:2610.26264v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Centrosymmetric nonsymmorphic AB2 (A = Ru, Os) borides exhibit anomalous superconducting properties, but the microscopic nature of their superconducting ground state remains unresolved. Here, we investigate RuB2 using zero- and transverse-field muon spin relaxation (muSR), first-principles electronic-structure calculations, and Ginzburg-Landau symmetry analysis. Zero-field muSR reveals spontaneous internal magnetic fields below Tc = 1.6 K, providing direct evidence for time-reversal symmetry breaking (TRSB). Transverse-field measurements establish a fully gapped superconducting state, with a gap magnitude close to the BCS weak-coupling limit. Density-functional calculations show that RuB2 is a multiband Z2 topological metal with appreciable spin-orbit coupling. Guided by the experimental observations and the symmetry of the orthorhombic crystal structure, our Ginzburg-Landau analysis suggests that an internally antisymmetric, nonunitary spin-triplet state is a promising candidate for the superconducting order parameter. This candidate is consistent with both the observed TRSB and the fully gapped superconducting state. These results establish RuB2 as a promising platform for investigating unconventional superconductivity arising from the interplay of crystal symmetry, multiband electronic structure, and spin-orbit coupling, and motivate further studies of related nonsymmorphic borides. The two muSR measurements provide complementary constraints: zero-field data probe spontaneous magnetism associated with TRSB, while transverse-field data characterize superconducting field distribution and support a full gap. The topological electronic structure and spin-orbit coupling provide context for unconventional pairing. Although these results do not uniquely determine the order parameter, they narrow candidates and highlight that nonsymmorphic symmetry may shape superconductivity in RuB2.



[109]. [*Finite-size scaling of spin-wave scattering and enhancement in magnonic Fabry-P\'erot nanoresonators*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26277 "Finite-size scaling of spin-wave scattering and enhancement in magnonic Fabry-P\'erot nanoresonators")

Anton Lutsenko, Sebastiaan van Dijken

arXiv:2610.26277v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Magnonic neural networks require compact resonant nodes that scatter spin waves efficiently, concentrate spin-wave energy and can be reconfigured. Hybrid magnonic Fabry-P\'erot resonators, formed by a ferromagnetic-metal stripe dipolar-coupled to a yttrium iron garnet (YIG) film, provide low-loss and programmable control of spin-wave transmission, but have so far been studied only for stripes that are long compared with the spin-wave wavelength. Here we use micromagnetic simulations to determine how the response of a 200-nm-wide CoFeB stripe on a 55-nm-thick YIG film evolves as the stripe length is reduced from infinity to 640 nm. An infinitely long stripe acts as a one-dimensional cavity that opens a transmission gap at the first-order resonance (1.70 GHz and 1.47 GHz for the parallel and antiparallel magnetic configuration) and enhances the out-of-plane precession amplitude in the YIG film by factors of about 5 and 12, without distorting the transmitted wavefront. For lengths of a few micrometers, backward-volume spin waves emitted from the stripe ends modulate the precession along the stripe and fragment the reflected and transmitted wavefronts. Below about 1.2 micrometer, the resonator scatters incident spin waves over the entire isofrequency contour of the film, with caustic beams that are most pronounced in the antiparallel configuration. In this regime the parallel configuration supports weakly enhanced quantized modes, whereas the antiparallel configuration supports a single mode with an out-of-plane enhancement of about 15. The width, thickness and shape of the stripe tune this mode by 50-100 MHz, and its strong enhancement lowers the onset of nonlinear frequency shift and fold-over to incident amplitudes of a few percent of the saturation magnetization. Sub-micrometer magnonic resonators are therefore promising reconfigurable nonlinear nodes for magnonic networks.



[110]. [*Hidden piezo-Hall effect*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26293 "Hidden piezo-Hall effect")

San-Dong Guo, Shi-Hao Zhang, Bingwen Zhang

arXiv:2610.26293v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Extensive studies of hidden spin polarization have established a broad class of hidden phenomena in quantum materials. Here, we introduce the hidden piezo-Hall effect, in which strain induces finite anomalous Hall conductivities (AHCs) in individual sectors while the global AHC remains zero. At zero strain, neither the full system nor its sectors exhibit an anomalous Hall effect (AHE). Under uniaxial strain, however, opposite sector AHCs emerge while their sum remains canceled. We establish this effect for a $d$-wave hidden altermagnet using a tight-binding model and first-principles calculations for the experimentally synthesized material $\mathrm{RbV_2Te_2O}$. Rotating the strain direction from the $x$ to the $y$ axis reverses the signs of the sector AHCs without generating a net Hall response. The same symmetry mechanism may also give rise to hidden piezomagneto-optical, piezo-thermal Hall, piezo-Nernst, and piezo-Ettingshausen effects. Our work extends hidden physics to strain-controlled transport and identifies $\mathrm{RbV_2Te_2O}$ as a promising candidate for experimental verification.



[111]. [*Machine Learning-Assisted Discovery of Carbon-Related Defect Formation Mechanisms in Diamond*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26301 "Machine Learning-Assisted Discovery of Carbon-Related Defect Formation Mechanisms in Diamond")

Grace Huang, Christopher Fivecoat, Xun Xu, Mengen Wang

arXiv:2610.26301v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Diamond is an important host material for functional defects such as solid-state spin qubits. During the defect creation by irradiation and subsequent thermal annealing, carbon-related defects, including vacancies and interstitials, are generated and can migrate to form new defect complexes. To investigate this kinetically driven process and reveal the formation mechanisms of the di-interstitials and di-vacancies in the neutral charge state, we fine-tuned a universal machine learning interatomic potential using a small dataset generated from density functional theory calculations. We obtained a barrier of 1.84 eV for mono-interstitial migration and found that two initially separated mono-interstitials only need to overcome a lower barrier to form di-interstitial configurations.
