Beyond the training data, the MLIP also identified the formation pathway for an additional di-interstitial configuration and a stepwise divacancy dissociation mechanism. These results reveal migration-assisted defect complex formation mechanisms and demonstrate how targeted MLIP training can identify reaction pathways.



[112]. [*La14.33(CN3)8N, a Nitridocarbonate based on the (CN3)5- Ion*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26307 "La14.33(CN3)8N, a Nitridocarbonate based on the (CN3)5- Ion")

Markus Str\"obele, Mykhailo Brytskyi, Carl P. Romao, Scott Kroeker, Hans-J\"urgen Meyer

arXiv:2610.26307v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Metal nitridocarbonates containing the (NCN)2- ion are established for a multitude of metal atoms throughout the periodic table. The preparation of nitridocarbonate compounds containing a (CN3)5-, (C2N4)6-, or (C3N6)6- ion remains challenging, although corresponding species have been established. The new compound La14.33(CN3)8N was synthesized in a conventional solid-state metathesis reaction from a mixture of LaF3, Na2(CN2) and Li3N in an open system at 550 {\deg}C. The reaction conditions, including the formation and de-composition of La14.33(CN3)8N are analyzed on basis of thermoanalytic studies. The crystal structure of La14.33(CN3)8N was refined by single-crystal X-ray diffraction, and the mid-IR frequencies resulting from two crystallographically distinct (CN3)5- ions in the structure are reported. 23Na, 7Li and 13C NMR studies provide additional support for the identity of the compound. The crystal structure of La14.33(CN3)8N is isotypic to that of Ce15(BN3)8N.



[113]. [*Poisson-shot-noise induced escape dynamics of a passive particle and a dimer*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26316 "Poisson-shot-noise induced escape dynamics of a passive particle and a dimer")

Gemeit Goel, Rahul Marathe

arXiv:2610.26316v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Mean First Passage Time (MFPT) is a crucial quantity that characterizes stochastic processes in various stochastic dynamical systems across different disciplines. The inverse of the MFPT, known as the escape rate, is helpful in the context of Brownian motion and chemical processes. The MFPT dynamics of a passive particle in the presence of active noises presents a fascinating avenue. In this work, we study the dynamics of two different systems: first, a single Brownian particle and second, a dimer (two Brownian particles connected by a spring). In both cases, the systems are kept in contact with a thermal bath and an active bath, and are trapped in a bistable potential. The active bath produces kicks that follow the Poisson distribution. The MFPT of the particle and the dimer is investigated in various scenarios. Analytical results are presented for the small kick variance approximation only. In this approximation, we formulate the MFPT in two scaling regimes: a weak-noise regime characterized by classical Arrhenius scaling for escape rate and a strong-noise regime in which the MFPT scales as a power law in the effective noise strength. For the general case, the problem is solved numerically. Next, under the same approximation, the MFPT of a dimer in the small-bond-strength regime is studied using a sequential-hopping mechanism, and in the large-bond-strength regime, the MFPT is formulated using the adiabatic elimination approach. Motivated by recent studies on speeding up the escape mechanism, the MFPT of the particle and the dimer in the presence of an intermediate barrier is also examined. We deduced from the numerical results that the MFPT can be enhanced and further optimized by selecting a suitable intermediate potential profile.



[114]. [*High-order resonances and emergent coherent dynamics in interacting Stark chains*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26328 "High-order resonances and emergent coherent dynamics in interacting Stark chains")

Jiahe Chang, Hongzheng Zhao

arXiv:2610.26328v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: A linear potential gradient can strongly suppress quantum dynamics, while interactions can activate resonant many-body processes whose higher-order structure remains largely unexplored. Here we uncover a systematic hierarchy of high-order interaction--tilt resonances in an interacting Stark chain, where the resonance order counts the minimal number of elementary exchange steps involved. These resonances manifest as long-lived coherent oscillations with order-dependent timescales. By isolating individual resonant channels and developing a microscopic effective description, we connect these dynamical timescales to the underlying many-body exchange processes and reveal a pronounced dependence on the initial-state structure. Our results provide crucial insights into high-order many-body resonances and show how commensurate interaction-to-tilt ratios generate a hierarchy of parametrically slow coherent many-body dynamics.



[115]. [*Investigating relative population partitioning in the presence of B$^-_\text{C}$ defects in diamond*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26332 "Investigating relative population partitioning in the presence of B$^-_\text{C}$ defects in diamond")

Rodrick Kuate Defo, Steven L. Richardson

arXiv:2610.26332v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Leveraging our earlier formalism for charge-state decay [see R. Kuate Defo et al., 108 235208 (2023)], we investigate relative population partitioning in the presence of singly negatively charged substitutional boron impurities (B$^-_{\rm C}$) in diamond samples, where these diamond samples also contain substitutional nitrogen (N$_{\rm C }$) impurities and have ether-terminated surfaces. Effectively, given any two defects, one donor and one acceptor, such that their charge-transition levels, their relative populations, and the temperature are known, the timescale for charge transfer between the two defects is completely determined. We use this insight and experimental measurements for the timescale for charge transfer between NV$^-$ centers and N$_{\rm C}$ hybridized with the ether-terminated surface of diamond, as well as the temperature and the relative populations for the NV$^-$ centers, [see Z. Yuan et al., Phys. Rev. Res. 2, 033263 (2020)] to argue that an additional defect must be modulating the relative populations of N$_{\rm C}$ hybridized with the ether-terminated surface of diamond. The shifts in the local Fermi level for N$_{\rm C}$ hybridized with the ether-terminated surface of diamond are consistent with the additional defect being a substitutional boron impurity (B$_{\rm C}$). Ultimately, our results allow for the determination of local relative concentrations of ionized defects.



[116]. [*Origins of Faceted Anomalous Scaling*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26356 "Origins of Faceted Anomalous Scaling")

Maximilien Bernard, Jos\'e Luis Iguain, Alejandro Kolton, Alberto Rosso

arXiv:2610.26356v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Fluctuating interfaces are typically characterized by their global, spectral, and local roughness exponents. Faceted anomalous scaling, characterized by $\alpha_s>1$, $\alpha_s\neq\alpha$, and $\alpha_{\mathrm{loc}}=1$, owes its name to the tendency of such interfaces to develop long, nearly linear segments called facets. Its statistical origin, and whether facets are required, have remained unclear. We show that this exponent structure is determined by the spatial statistics of the slope field. Slope increments split into typical fluctuations and rare, larger jumps. This anomalous scaling arises either when the typical slope increment displays genuine system-size-dependent anomalous scaling or when the rare jumps dominate the second moment. The two contributions can coexist, and their competition leads to bifractal scaling of the slopes. We establish this framework analytically in the generalized anharmonic Larkin model and the columnar Kardar--Parisi--Zhang equation, and we derive exactly the global, spectral, and local roughness exponents in both cases. The Larkin model realizes both mechanisms and produces anomalous interfaces with or without facets, whereas columnar KPZ belongs to the jump-dominated case and yields $\alpha_s=3/2$.



[117]. [*Spatiotemporal Imaging of Thermally Driven Metal-Insulator Phase Domain Propagation in VO2*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26367 "Spatiotemporal Imaging of Thermally Driven Metal-Insulator Phase Domain Propagation in VO2")

Ilaria Delbono, Raouf Amara, Matias Feldman, Hankun Yang, C\'edric Leau, Carlo Scian, Erwan Dandeu, S\'ebastien Royer, Debora Pierucci, Emmanuel Lhuillier, Franck Vidal, Tiziana Cesca, Hilton B. de Aguiar, Jose Ordonez-Miranda, Dani\`ele Fournier, Raj Pandya, Boris Kalinic, Giovanni Mattei, James K. Utterback

arXiv:2610.26367v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Achieving a detailed picture of the nature and dynamics of phase boundaries remains a major challenge, particularly solid-solid transitions central to phase-change materials. Here, we use stroboscopic optical scattering microscopy and finite-element simulations to study the nonlinear thermally driven motion of the mesoscale insulator-metal phase boundary in VO2 thin films. We visualize the lateral spatiotemporal evolution of a diffraction-limited photoinduced metallic phase domain, resolving its outward propagation, contraction, and ultimate disappearance. The phase boundary dynamics are governed by heat flow, which can be controlled by excitation fluence, film thickness, and substrate thermal conductivity. Parameter-free agreement between the data and nonlinear thermal transport simulations identify the phase boundary transport as locally diffusive under this regime. We quantify how the latent heat acts as a thermal inertia, suppressing transport and prolonging the phase coexistence. Paradoxically, despite being driven by the underlying thermal gradient locally described by Fourier's law, the mesoscale phase front propagates faster than expected from thermal diffusion. This phenomenon arises from the distinction between temperature-field evolution and phase-boundary isotherm motion. These results establish a link between heat dissipation and mesoscale phase-front motion, providing a framework for controlling phase-transition kinetics relevant to photonics applications of VO2. We anticipate that this approach generalizes to a broad class of first- and second-order transitions.



[118]. [*An infinite sequence of higher order Ehrenfest transitions in a one-dimensional crystal*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26371 "An infinite sequence of higher order Ehrenfest transitions in a one-dimensional crystal")

Sakineh Mizani, Szabolcs Varga, Martin Oettel, P\'eter Gurin

arXiv:2610.26371v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Higher-order Ehrenfest phase transitions are exceptionally rare and poorly understood. We present an exactly solvable one-dimensional model exhibiting a sequence of Ehrenfest transitions of arbitrary $n\geq2$ order. These transitions occur without symmetry breaking and even the second-order transition lacks conventional critical behavior, although the density derivative of the correlation length diverges. Our results reveal a distinct class of noncritical phase transitions and help clarify the scope of the Ehrenfest classification.



[119]. [*Two-Dimensional Quantum Droplets in a Mixture of Bose-Einstein Condensates*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26449 "Two-Dimensional Quantum Droplets in a Mixture of Bose-Einstein Condensates")

Yue Zhang, Liangchao Chen, Wei Han, Linshan Gong, Yazhou Wang, Zekui Wang, Xinhao Liang, Wenjie Sui, Pengjun Wang, Lianghui Huang, Zengming Meng, Zhuxiong Ye, Jing Zhang

arXiv:2610.26449v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Quantum droplets represent a striking manifestation of beyond-mean-field physics, with properties that depend sensitively on dimensionality. Although quantum droplets have been experimentally realized in one and three dimensions, their realization in two dimensions has remained elusive. Here, we observe the formation of a two-dimensional (2D) quantum droplet in a quasi-2D geometry by transferring the single-component Bose-Einstein condensates (BECs) into a spin mixture in the Lee-Huang-Yang (LHY) regime, where the LHY effect evidently deviates from its three-dimensional form. We find that, in our scheme, 2D quantum droplets are more stable and exhibit longer lifetimes than previously reported quantum droplets. By instead transferring the initial BECs into another single-spin state with a negative scattering length, we generate 2D Townes solitons. A comparison between the two types of self-bound states shows that, whereas 2D Townes solitons are stable only near a critical atom number determined by the scattering length, 2D quantum droplets remain well-defined and self-bound over a much broader parameter range. These realizations of quantum droplets and Townes solitons in two dimensions provide new insights into self-bound states and open new avenues for exploring nonlinear dynamics in quantum liquids.



[120]. [*Tunable annealing of defects in self-aligning active crystals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26459 "Tunable annealing of defects in self-aligning active crystals")

Kanka Ghosh, Andreas M. Menzel

arXiv:2610.26459v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Obtaining defect-free single-crystals is of crucial importance in soft matter and materials science, spanning both fundamental interests and practical applications. Active crystals, encompassing diverse dense systems from living organisms to artificial metamaterials, can offer a promising platform for autonomous structural reorganization. We demonstrate that increasing spatiotemporal orientational fluctuations around the mean polarized state can enhance crystallographic order in active crystals. Poly-to-single-crystal transformations in defect-rich, self-aligned active Brownian crystals with coupled translational-rotational dynamics are investigated. We show that long-lived persistence with slow alignment controls the defect-annealing capability. By disentangling activity magnitude from associated timescales, we reveal activity-assisted annealing. Tuning the persistence length enables overcoming thermal energy barriers. Simultaneously adjusting persistence and alignment timescales can tune relaxation from `fast' to `slow', yielding defect-rich and defect-free crystals, respectively. Furthermore, active crystals of point particles are found to exhibit similar defect annealing when flocking is delayed by tuning the timescale of the velocity relaxation towards the polarized state. These findings suggest that the mechanism may extend to a broader class of active crystals. Our results intersect statistical physics of living soft matter with the mechanics of defects and microstructures, proposing a nonequilibrium route towards reconfigurable materials.



[121]. [*Non-local Stabilization and Synchronization of Larmor Precession in Trapped Polariton Condensate Dimer*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26499 "Non-local Stabilization and Synchronization of Larmor Precession in Trapped Polariton Condensate Dimer")

Stepan Baryshev, Alexey Yulin, Ivan Gnusov, Sergey Alyatkin, Pavlos G. Lagoudakis

arXiv:2610.26499v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We demonstrate mutual synchronization of Larmor precessions between spatially separated, optically trapped polariton condensates. We identify the cross-condensate correlation g(2)1,2({\tau}) as a sensitive metric for condensate coupling. Synchronization of precessions in a condensate dimer is mediated by polariton outflow and resonantly depends on spectral overlap and precession winding. All-optical stirring of one site non-locally increases the pseudo-spin dephasing time of its static neighbor, prolonging phase-locking in a dimer by an order of magnitude. Our work establishes a scalable platform for studying non-equilibrium time-periodically driven systems and for high-speed spinoptronic networks.



[122]. [*Atomic-level study of dose-dependent lattice thermal conductivity degradation in irradiated tungsten*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26502 "Atomic-level study of dose-dependent lattice thermal conductivity degradation in irradiated tungsten")

Mary K Chessey, Jos\'e Julio Guti\'errez Moreno, Sajjan Singh Mehta, Paolo Settembri, Arda Erbasan, Dominik Freinberger, Marina Garc\'ia Romero, Mervi J Mantsinen

arXiv:2610.26502v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Plasma-facing components (PFCs) in future fusion reactors must withstand extreme environments, making tungsten (W) a prime candidate material due to its high thermal conductivity, high melting temperature, low sputtering yield, low tritium absorption. However, neutron irradiation introduces complex nanostructural defects that severely degrade these critical properties. In this work, we use Non-Equilibrium Molecular Dynamics (NEMD) simulations coupled with the Creation Relaxation Algorithm (CRA) to investigate the lattice thermal conductivity (kappa) of W across a wide range of irradiation doses. Systems treated with different doses up to 1.8 canonical displacements per atom (cdpa) were simulated to generate and study diverse defect structures, including vacancies and self-interstitial atom (SIA) clusters. By correlating spatially-resolved temperature gradients with local defect concentrations, we show that even small changes in defect type and distribution significantly affect thermal transport. We employ Machine Learning (ML) models to develop a quantitative relationship between the defect features and the resulting thermal conductivity degradation. The ML analysis identifies vacancy density as the dominant structural factor controlling thermal transport. We found a significant, nonlinear suppression of kappa with increasing defect density. Predictive multi-defect mapping built upon our ML model resolved a step-like degradation transition at a critical vacancy threshold of 0.05 at.%, beyond which phonon scattering completely saturated and capped the maximum relative lattice thermal conductivity degradation at approximately 30 at.%. This integrated simulation and ML approach provides a powerful, predictive framework for quantifying the complex relationship between structure and thermal conductivity in irradiated fusion materials.



[123]. [*Modular Landau Analysis of Coupled Phase Transitions: Application to Ferroelectric Liquid Crystals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26505 "Modular Landau Analysis of Coupled Phase Transitions: Application to Ferroelectric Liquid Crystals")

Sisi Guan, Yucen Han, Lei Zhang

arXiv:2610.26505v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In systems with coupled order parameters, the relaxation of existing order can modify subsequent transitions, while cooperation and competition between emerging modes can produce complex phase sequences. We develop a modular Landau analysis that locally relaxes noncritical parent-phase variables to obtain effective one- and two-mode free energies. The resulting coefficients quantify relaxation-induced changes in transition characteristics and mode coupling, including changes in transition order for even-symmetry modes. The resulting modules are assembled successively along parent branches, and the equilibrium phase sequence is selected by comparing the fully relaxed free energies of the admissible branches. We apply the framework to ferroelectric liquid crystals, where orientational, polar, smectic, and tilt orders interact and give rise to multiple competing pathways, using a uniaxial model with longitudinal polarization. We fit the model to the measurements reported by Strachan et al. [Adv. Sci. 12(3), 2409754 (2025)]. A single thermodynamic parameter set reproduces the observed transition temperatures, transition types, and phase sequence, while capturing the main birefringence trend. Smectic layer spacing is reconstructed separately through a fitted observation map based on the calculated equilibrium tilt.



[124]. [*Exploring ground state phase diagrams of Rydberg atom arrays with finite projected entangled-pair states*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26520 "Exploring ground state phase diagrams of Rydberg atom arrays with finite projected entangled-pair states")

Konrad Schr\"oder, Erik Lennart Weerda, Matteo Rizzi

arXiv:2610.26520v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Rydberg atom arrays (RAA) have been advocated as a promising avenue for the quantum simulation of different exotic quantum states. Numerical predictions for the ground state properties of this experimental platform can, however, be challenging in two-dimensional setups. In particular, faithfully treating long-range interactions is often crucial for distinguishing the fate of competing phases, but it usually comes at considerable cost. Projected entangled-pair states (PEPS), optimized via a sampling-based approach, have been shown to deal with such terms on the same ground as shorter-range ones. In this work, we employ these tools to investigate debated physical questions in different frustrated geometries of RAA, from the emergence of spin-glass physics on the Kagom\'e lattice to topological order beyond the PXP model on the Ruby lattice. By that, we contribute to establishing PEPS as well-suited tools for creating digital twins of RAA experiments.



[125]. [*Exact cost of the Euclidean Maximum Traveling Salesman Problem in one dimension*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26536 "Exact cost of the Euclidean Maximum Traveling Salesman Problem in one dimension")

Sergio Caracciolo, Carlo Vanoni

arXiv:2610.26536v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We study the random Euclidean Maximum Traveling Salesman Problem: finding a Hamiltonian cycle of maximal total cost through any set of $N$ cities on a line, where each edge cost is a function of its Euclidean length. We characterize the maximizing cycles for convex increasing, concave increasing, and convex decreasing cost functions. For points sampled independently and uniformly from the unit interval, we derive explicit expressions for the average maximal cost for arbitrary $N$ and asymptotic expansions with power-law edge costs.



[126]. [*Coexistence phase of the two-dimensional Vicsek model*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26561 "Coexistence phase of the two-dimensional Vicsek model")

Yu Duan, Hugues Chat\'{e}, Beno\^it Mahault, Xia-qing Shi

arXiv:2610.26561v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The Vicsek model continues to be central in statistical physics studies of active matter, with major recent surprises such as the partial metastability of the famous Toner-Tu flocking phase. Here we report on the diversity of band patterns observed in its coexistence phase --some of them never observed before--, but we also argue that most disappear in the infinite-size limit, leaving nevertheless a different picture than anticipated previously. We finally show that these rather regular, globally ordered band configurations can be destroyed by a localized perturbation: the coexistence phase is also at least partially metastable, and thus must be, asymptotically, a disordered phase made of a variety of chaotically interacting traveling bands.



[127]. [*Phase Diagram of the Two-Dimensional Vicsek Model*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26569 "Phase Diagram of the Two-Dimensional Vicsek Model")

Yu Duan, Hugues Chat\'{e}, Beno\^it Mahault, Xia-qing Shi

arXiv:2610.26569v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Motivated by gaps in our current knowledge of the Vicsek model, we present a major update of its phase diagram in two space dimensions, summarizing comprehensive numerical simulations at length- and timescales never explored before. Our main conclusions are that multiple phase separation scenarios are at play, not just the simple microphase separation put forward in [Phys. Rev. Lett. 114, 068101 (2015)], and that the coexistence phase is subdivided in two. We also map out the region of metastability of the homogeneous Toner-Tu liquid phase.



[128]. [*Avalanches and Aging Driven by Parametric Amplification in Kerr Oscillator Networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26573 "Avalanches and Aging Driven by Parametric Amplification in Kerr Oscillator Networks")

Alejandro S. G\'omez, Javier del Pino

arXiv:2610.26573v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Gain, loss, and nonlinearity shape the phase-space landscape of driven-dissipative systems, such as Kerr parametric oscillator (KPO) networks realized in superconducting, nanomechanical, and photonic arrays. Networks of KPOs exploit the stationary states of this landscape for Ising machines, bosonic error-correcting codes, and sensing. Yet how transients reach these states from a given initial condition remains largely unexplored. Here we show that KPO networks with nonlinear couplings can develop avalanches in their transient dynamics: rapid cascades of amplification between modes reminiscent of the relaxation avalanches of glassy systems. The mechanism is that parametric gain first activates the modes whose detuning it can overcome, and their nonlinear frequency shifts then pull dormant modes into the gain window one at a time. Dormant modes keep decaying while they wait for their turn, so every activation starts from a smaller seed and the intervals between activations grow geometrically. The network thus ages: the longer it has evolved, the slower it changes, and activations accumulate only logarithmically in time. In a 20-mode network, each dwell lasts about twice as long as the previous one. While aging delays the approach to the stationary state, additive noise sets a floor on the seeds and unsticks the network, shortening the longest dwells first while the activation sequence survives to noise several orders of magnitude stronger.



[129]. [*Probing the physical origins of 1/f noise by nonlinear transport*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26592 "Probing the physical origins of 1/f noise by nonlinear transport")

Nicolas Fontaine, Bertrand Reulet

arXiv:2610.26592v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We report measurements of low frequency noise in the nonlinear electron transport of tunnel junctions that are dc or ac biased. We show that not only the resistance but also the nonlinear coefficients exhibit $1/f$-like noise, and that correlations among them exist. Using the common theoretical model for electron transport in tunnel junctions, we relate our measurements to fluctuations of barrier heights. We show in particular that the barrier heights on either side of the junction have strong fluctuations that are almost perfectly anti-correlated.



[130]. [*From particles to continuum theories in effective models of self-propelled rods*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26593 "From particles to continuum theories in effective models of self-propelled rods")

Elia Bronzo, Hugues Chat\'e, Beno\^it Mahault, Alexandre Solon

arXiv:2610.26593v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Models of colliding self-propelled rods are abstractions of many real systems such as gliding bacteria and biofilaments displaced by molecular motors. They have been studied mostly in two space dimensions and shown to display a wealth of different collective regimes with many parameters involved in the ensuing phase diagrams. Continuum-level theories faithful to this phenomenology are largely lacking since coarse-graining models of explicit rods is notoriously difficult. Here we study collections of particles in which the rods geometry is taken into account effectively, by implementing the nematic alignment of their velocities and two types of repulsion. These Vicsek-style models are simple enough to be starting points for systematic coarse-graining. We thus derive hydrodynamic equations that we show to have solutions qualitatively faithful to their particle-level origin.



[131]. [*Strain-induced disorder from Schottky gates on high mobility 2DEGs*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26607 "Strain-induced disorder from Schottky gates on high mobility 2DEGs")

Sambit Mohapatra, Lukas Berg, Jean-Baptiste Cuvellier, Yan Sun, Abdelhanin Aassime, Alan Durnez, Antonella Cavanna, Dominique Mailly, Ulf Gennser

arXiv:2610.26607v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Electrostatic gates on two-dimensional electron gases (2DEGs) in GaAs/AlGaAs heterostructures are essential for numerous studies of correlation driven phenomena, many performed within the fractional quantum Hall effect. However, the most commonly used type of gates, the metallic Schottky gate, has the propensity to adversely impact the electron mobility and suppress the fractional quantum Hall states. Here we demonstrate through commensurability oscillations under specially designed nanostructured superlattice gates, that strain effects are responsible for the mobility reduction. The experimental results agree well with COMSOL Multiphysics simulations. In a regular, unpatterned gate the disordered, granular nature of the metal film fills the same role as the superlattice in creating a strain field penetrating down to the 2DEG, as we show with a simplified model. These results will help in understanding, improving and finding novel methods to reduce strain-induced disorder in gated high-mobility 2DEGs.



[132]. [*Stabilization of Ferroelectric Domains via Interfacial Strain in HoFeO3- BaTiO3 Nanofiber Heterostructures for Enhanced Hybrid Energy Harvesting*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26611 "Stabilization of Ferroelectric Domains via Interfacial Strain in HoFeO3- BaTiO3 Nanofiber Heterostructures for Enhanced Hybrid Energy Harvesting")

Smita Chaturvedi, Swathi Krishna, Shivshankar Jokare, Supriya Sahoo, Vikash Kushwaha, Priyank Shyam, Mandar Shirolkar, R. Boomishankar

arXiv:2610.26611v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Piezoelectric nanogenerators (PENGs) are an attractive platform for self-powered mechanical-energy harvesting, but their output is often limited by depolarization fields, charge leakage, and inefficient stress transfer to the active dipoles, particularly in polymer-dominated composites. Here we report HoFeO3-BaTiO3 (HFO-BTO) nanofiber heterostructures prepared by alternating sol-gel electrospinning, in which orthorhombic HoFeO3 (Pbnm) and tetragonal BaTiO3 (P4mm) coexist in intimate contact. Rietveld-refined X-ray diffraction, high-resolution transmission electron microscopy, and Raman spectroscopy indicate interfacial lattice distortion. Polarization-electric field loops show that the heterostructure retains a polarization comparable to that of BaTiO3 (16.39 vs 15.80 microcoulombs per square centimeter at zero field) while its switching-current maximum moves to a higher field (about 7 vs about 3.5 kilovolts per centimeter), consistent with strain-stabilized ferroelectric domains. When the nanofibers are encapsulated in polydimethylsiloxane (PDMS) and excited under cyclic impact, the device produces a hybrid output: the piezoelectric response of the strained BaTiO3 is supplemented by a triboelectric contribution from contact electrification. The HFO-BTO device delivers open-circuit voltage peaks of about +6 volts and -8 volts and a power density of 24.31 microwatts per cubic centimeter at a 4.7 megaohm load. This is 2.4 times the value for an identically fabricated BaTiO3 device (10.00 microwatts per cubic centimeter) and 8.7 times that of the HoFeO3 device (2.78 microwatts per cubic centimeter). These results link interface-driven lattice distortion to improved electromechanical output and identify all-oxide nanofiber heterostructures as a promising route to lead-free energy harvesting.



[133]. [*Statistical decision theory in thermodynamics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26617 "Statistical decision theory in thermodynamics")

Yoshihiko Hasegawa

arXiv:2610.26617v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We introduce statistical decision theory in stochastic thermodynamic systems. Statistical decision theory integrates several statistical tasks, such as estimation, hypothesis testing, and classification. In this study, we consider statistical decision theory in stochastic thermodynamics, which unifies sensing, classification, and uncertainty relations. In particular, we focus on the minimax risk, the smallest achievable expected loss under the worst-case parameter choice, even with an optimal estimator. We derive lower bounds on the minimax risk using Le Cam's two-point method. The derived relationships can be used across a broader range of thermodynamic scenarios. To illustrate these relations, we study classification and distribution estimation problems in a Markov process. We demonstrate that the minimax risk provides lower bounds given by the dynamical activity in these problems. Furthermore, by examining particular dynamics, we establish that the resulting bounds simplify to uncertainty relations involving dynamical activity or entropy production.



[134]. [*Nucleation-type criticality with infinite number of free-energy minima beyond mean-field theory*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26638 "Nucleation-type criticality with infinite number of free-energy minima beyond mean-field theory")

Jun Takahashi, Yoshihiko Nishikawa

arXiv:2610.26638v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We study the phase diagram of a three-dimensional Heisenberg model with Dzyaloshinski--Moriya interaction under the presence of Potts-like anisotropy. The phase transition between the winding phase and the ferromagnetically ordered phase exhibits a critical phase transition with anomalous features such as critical exponents consistent with that of a first-order transition while maintaining continuity of physical quantities. Furthermore, at the critical point, we see a growing number of free energy local minima, supporting the picture of infinitely many discontinuous transitions forming criticality. Our numerical result unify de Gennes' theory of nucleation-type transitions and Dzyaloshinskii's calculation of chiral magnets by demonstrating that such anomalous criticality can be realized in microscopic models beyond mean-field.



[135]. [*Variational Block Correlated Coupled Cluster Monte Carlo*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26646 "Variational Block Correlated Coupled Cluster Monte Carlo")

Hunter Brodie, Alexander Shilcusky, Adrian E. Feiguin

arXiv:2610.26646v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Motivated by the recent success of quantum neural states, we present a variational implementation of the coupled cluster method suitable for quantum Monte Carlo simulations. We introduce a Young tableaux representation of the wave-function coefficients and their derivatives that substantially reduces the computational complexity of the variational optimization. While the algorithm is generic, we illustrate its implementation with an application to the Heisenberg model in one and two dimensions, using the block correlated coupled cluster ansatz (BCCC) with doubles and triples. Convergence is greatly improved by means of importance sampling based on a fictitious temperature. The resulting approach achieves competitive accuracy while providing a scalable framework for variational coupled-cluster calculations.



[136]. [*Dynamic clustering of active chemotactic Janus particles*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26657 "Dynamic clustering of active chemotactic Janus particles")

Jo\"el Mabillard, Fr\'ed\'eric van Wijland, Jean-Baptiste Fournier

arXiv:2610.26657v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Modeling self-propulsion as an emergent diffusiophoretic effect, we come back to the dynamic clustering experiment of active Janus colloids. Working in the original three-dimensional geometry, and using the chemical solute concentration as a control parameter, we find that the dynamic clustering region occupies a robust fraction of the phase diagram. This behavior is rationalized using a linear stability analysis that efficiently captures the instability of the gas phase to dynamic clusters. We also find that dynamic clustering results from the combined effects of a Keller-Segel collapse and of a motility-induced phase separation, either being arrested by the orientational coupling to the chemical field in agreement with previous work.



[137]. [*Vacancy and Helium-Cluster Thermomigration in Tungsten*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26667 "Vacancy and Helium-Cluster Thermomigration in Tungsten")

Sanad Alturk, Enrique Martinez

arXiv:2610.26667v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Strong thermal gradients in tungsten plasma-facing components can bias the migration of irradiation-induced defects and thereby alter their redistribution, interaction, and retention. Here, we extend an atomistic jump-statistics framework previously developed for hydrogen to quantify the thermomigration of a single vacancy and interstitial He$_1$--He$_4$ clusters in body-centered-cubic tungsten. Non-equilibrium molecular-dynamics simulations performed over several thermal gradients are used to determine the directional jump ratio, while geometrically derived projected jump lengths convert the fitted directional-bias coefficient into the heat-of-transport coefficient $Q^*=\alpha kT^2$, where $k$ is the Boltzmann constant, $T$ the temperature, and $\alpha$ a constant. All investigated defects exhibit negative values of $\alpha$, corresponding to net migration toward the hot region. He$_4$ has the largest response, $\alpha=-2.2457\times10^{-2}$ K$^{-1}$, followed closely by the vacancy, whereas He$_3$ exhibits the weakest response despite its intermediate cluster size. Independent constant-temperature simulations over 1000--1600 K show that He$_3$ is also the least mobile cluster and has the largest effective migration energy, $E_m^{\mathrm{eff}}=0.271$ eV. In contrast, He$_4$ is more mobile than He$_3$ and has the smallest effective migration energy, 0.087 eV. Agreement between the He$_3$ concentration profile predicted from the jump statistics and that obtained from a long molecular-dynamics trajectory provides an independent validation of the method. These results show that thermomigration cannot be inferred from cluster size, diffusivity, or migration energy alone and provide defect-specific heat-of-transport parameters for higher-length-scale models of tungsten under fusion-relevant thermal gradients.



[138]. [*Elastic theory of ideal liquid crystal elastomers*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26688 "Elastic theory of ideal liquid crystal elastomers")

Zumrat Usmanova, Ruobing Bai

arXiv:2610.26688v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: A liquid crystal elastomer (LCE) exhibits a two-way coupling between its mesogen order and network elasticity: the mesogen order sets the undeformed network conformation, and the network deformation reorients the mesogens. This coupling gives rise to large, reversible stimuli-responsive actuation, as well as to soft elasticity, a finite deformation at nearly zero stress. This article reviews the elastic theory of ideal LCEs, bridging single-chain statistics and continuum constitutive models. The "ideal" elastic behavior rests on the assumption of anisotropic Gaussian chains that retain no memory of the crosslinking state, such that rotating the director costs no free energy. The corresponding neo-classical free energy of a monodomain LCE is non-quasi-convex, with a continuous family of stress-free ground states, leading to thermally induced spontaneous deformation and soft elasticity accommodated by coexisting stripe domains. The quasi-convex relaxation of the free energy classifies the behavior of polydomain LCEs into three phases, each with a distinct macroscopic mechanical response depending on the stretch state. The ideal elastic theory can be further extended to incorporate additional behaviors and physical processes, including thermo- and photomechanical coupling, Frank elasticity from director distortion, semi-soft or non-soft behavior arising from network nonideality, and temperature- and rate-dependent viscoelasticity.



[139]. [*Proximity Transient Absorption in van der Waals Heterostructures*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26715 "Proximity Transient Absorption in van der Waals Heterostructures")

Neema Rafizadeh, Ting Zheng, Hui Zhao

arXiv:2610.26715v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Transient absorption spectroscopy is a widely used technique for investigating ultrafast electronic dynamics, where transient optical signals are generally interpreted as arising from excitations within the material hosting the probed optical transition. A distinct form of transient absorption spectroscopy, termed proximity transient absorption (PTA), is demonstrated, in which the probed optical transition and the monitored photoexcited carriers reside in different materials. Using graphene/hBN/WS$_2$ heterostructures, photoexcited carriers in graphene induce a strong transient optical response at the excitonic resonance of a WS$_2$ monolayer through Coulomb screening, even when the two layers remain electronically isolated by an atomically thin hBN spacer. The resulting signal follows the carrier dynamics of graphene rather than those of WS$_2$, demonstrating that the WS$_2$ exciton acts as an optical transducer of electronic processes in a neighboring material. The signal decreases systematically with increasing hBN thickness, confirming its proximity origin while remaining substantial for separations approaching 2 nm. PTA provides a noncontact approach for optically modulating one material using photoexcited carriers in another and enables probing of carrier dynamics in materials that are otherwise difficult to investigate directly. More broadly, PTA establishes a new modality of ultrafast spectroscopy and reveals that transient optical signals in van der Waals heterostructures can originate from excitations residing in spatially separated layers.



[140]. [*Collective diffusion of anisotropic hard-particle mixtures*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26721 "Collective diffusion of anisotropic hard-particle mixtures")

Anouar El Moumane, G. H. Philipp Nguyen, Klaus Mecke, Hartmut L\"owen, Ren\'e Wittmann

arXiv:2610.26721v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Predicting the dynamics of anisotropic mixtures of different species is a challenging theoretical task. We show that Dynamical Density Functional Theory (DDFT), a theory predicting time evolution of the one-particle-density through a free energy functional, can be simplified to a diffusion equation. To quantify the dynamical relaxation behavior, we deduce corrections to the regular long-time collective diffusion coefficients from the direct correlations between the interacting particles and determine these effective diffusion coefficients analytically for a number of hard-particle systems, including mixtures of hard spheres, as well as isotropic and nematic phases of uniaxial spherocylinders and biaxial spherotriangles. Finally, we discuss how to generalize our approach to systems with positional order and suggest a possible application to predicting relaxation times of orientational order parameters.



[141]. [*Phase-sensitive quantum-state texture during thermalization of a Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya Heisenberg dimer*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26736 "Phase-sensitive quantum-state texture during thermalization of a Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya Heisenberg dimer")

Onofre Rojas, Zhirayr Adamyan, Fadwa Benabdallah, Vadim Ohanyan

arXiv:2610.26736v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate quantum-state texture during the thermal relaxation of an anisotropic spin-$1/2$ Heisenberg dimer subject to a longitudinal magnetic field and a Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya (DM) interaction. Starting from local transverse and longitudinal couplings to a bosonic bath, we construct a global weak-coupling Lindblad equation, identify its transition channels, and discuss the applicability and limitations of the secular approximation. The Gibbs state is obtained explicitly in the local spin basis, revealing that the DM coupling controls both the modulus and phase of the single-excitation coherence by modifying the energy splitting and rotating the chiral phase. For heating and cooling protocols, we compare rugosity, fidelity-based texture, and trace-distance texture with coherence, vector spin chirality, and purity. The relaxation dynamics separates population thermalization from the decay and precession of deliberately prepared energy-basis coherence. Their combined contribution to local coherence produces DM-dependent texture overshoots and, during cooling, damped oscillations and sign changes of the coherence quadratures. These transients differ from the evolution of coherence magnitude and purity, while trace-distance texture weights them differently from the overlap-based quantifiers. Calculations at reduced dissipation preserve the texture maxima within the secular model. The results identify a phase-sensitive diagnostic of relaxation.



[142]. [*A McLennan formula for equiaccessible steady states*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26755 "A McLennan formula for equiaccessible steady states")

Jacob Calvert

arXiv:2610.26755v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Near equilibrium, the Boltzmann distribution is corrected by the transient irreversible entropy flux, a result known as the McLennan formula. By expanding around equiaccessible steady states instead, we derive an analogue that can hold arbitrarily far from equilibrium. At zeroth order, steady-state occupation is determined solely by local escape rates, yielding a strong form of the active-matter principle of "low rattling." The first-order correction measures the impact of traffic imbalances on the accessibility of each state, marking the onset of nonlocal determinants of relative occupation.



[143]. [*Capillary forces between two identical spherical particles*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26806 "Capillary forces between two identical spherical particles")

Stefano Onofri, Thejas Hulikal Chakrapani, Stefan Luding, Elke Scholten, Ahmed Jarray, Wouter K. den Otter

arXiv:2610.26806v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The properties of colloidal suspensions and granular matter change drastically upon adding a small amount of liquid capable of forming capillary bridges between the solid particles. The attractive forces exerted by these liquid bridges induce the formation of particle aggregates and networks, which radically changes the macroscopic mechanical and rheological behaviour. Predicting these forces across bridge sizes, particle separations and wettability is therefore central to particle-scale modelling of capillary suspensions and wet granular matter. While the Young-Laplace equation governing the properties of the liquid bridges is well known, it lacks a convenient analytical solution. Current simulations therefore employ approximate analytic expressions, typically developed for contact angles below ~40{\deg} and bridge volumes below ~2.5% of the solid particle's volume. We compare several frequently used expressions from the literature with numerical solutions of the theoretical capillary forces. An overall good agreement is observed in the aforementioned parameter ranges, while the approximate expressions start to deviate outside their intended domains. We propose accurate and computationally efficient expressions for the capillary force that model this behaviour, for contact angles from 5{\deg} to 80{\deg} and bridge volumes from $10^{-6}$ to 0.15. We also explore mesoscopic 'fluid-scale' simulations of bridges by Multi-body Dissipative Particle Dynamics (MDPD), to ascertain whether this coarse-grained fluid model faithfully captures the continuum capillary forces. Remarkably, a good agreement is observed even for liquid bridges described by a mere few dozen fluid particles. Together, these results offer two approaches, at different resolution scales, for simulating bridges over a wide range of bridge volumes and contact angles.



[144]. [*Kondo Lattice model for field induced triplet pairing in CeRh$_2$As$_2$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26858 "Kondo Lattice model for field induced triplet pairing in CeRh$_2$As$_2$")

Indra Gankhuyag, Piers Coleman

arXiv:2610.26858v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Spin triplet superconductors are of interest because of their topological properties, and their robustness to high magnetic fields. CeRh$_2$As$_2$ is a unique heavy fermion superconductor with both even-parity and candidate odd-parity superconducting phases with a special feature enabling a field induced phase transition between the two. The field induced transition which switches the parity of the Cooper pairs is enabled by a staggered antisymmetric spin orbit coupling (ASOC) interaction. However, the heavy fermion origins of the superconductivity in CeRh$_2$As$_2$ and the origin of the antisymmetric spin-orbit coupling are currently unexplored. Here, we develop a Kondo lattice model for CeRh$_2$As$_2$ and demonstrate how local hybridization between Cerium $d$- and $f$-orbitals, driven by the locally broken inversion symmetry, at the Cerium sites generates the ASOC. The resulting Kondo lattice model leads to nearly degenerate even and odd parity superconducting states that display a field induced parity transition between an even-parity state and an odd-parity pseudospin triplet state. We discuss the wider implications of our results extended to other heavy fermion systems like CeSiI.



[145]. [*Boundary-Sensitive Quantum Order on Non-Amenable Lattices*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26859 "Boundary-Sensitive Quantum Order on Non-Amenable Lattices")

Xingzhi Wang, Zohar Nussinov, Gerardo Ortiz

arXiv:2610.26859v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We show that quantum systems on non-amenable lattices, whose boundaries constitute an extensive fraction of the system, host novel forms of bulk ordering with no Euclidean counterpart. Combining boundary-retaining iDMRG with an exact symmetry-fixing duality, we investigate $p<5$ state clock models on the coordination-3 infinite Cayley tree. We find that between the ordered and disordered phases lies an emergent intermediate phase with bulk order induced by an arbitrarily weak boundary field rather than by conventional spontaneous symmetry-breaking. This establishes boundary-sensitive order as a distinct quantum ordering mechanism defying Landau's spontaneous symmetry-breaking paradigm. The intermediate phase realizes a bulk-boundary correspondence in which the unpolarized boundary selects a specific ordered bulk state.



[146]. [*On Higher-Form Fracton Hydrodynamics with Momentum Conservation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26862 "On Higher-Form Fracton Hydrodynamics with Momentum Conservation")

Ben Bobell, Rahul Nandkishore, Andrew Lucas

arXiv:2610.26862v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Momentum conservation is common in fluids, yet it may be incompatible with the hydrodynamics of theories with exotic conservation laws (such as fracton hydrodynamics) unless symmetries are spontaneously broken. Using the Martin-Siggia-Rose (MSR) effective field theory formalism, we develop a representation-theoretic framework for the hydrodynamics of translationally invariant systems with exotic conservation laws without spontaneous symmetry breaking. Under mild assumptions on the form of hydrodynamics, we show that such theories must satisfy two representation theoretic consistency conditions and a generalized Gauss's law constraint. Higher-form symmetries satisfy these conditions and we derive their ideal hydrodynamics; in three dimensions, the one-form case produces ideal non-relativistic magnetohydrodynamics. Finally, we prove that no other theories are compatible with momentum conservation in SO(3) nor SO($d$), for sufficiently large odd $d$.



[147]. [*Constrained dissipation and the lifetime of quantum many-body scars*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22223 "Constrained dissipation and the lifetime of quantum many-body scars")

Weyner Ccuiro, Rosario Fazio, Alessandro Silva

arXiv:2610.22223v2 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We investigate the fate of quantum many-body scars in the PXP model subject to Markovian dissipation. We show that when local jump operators respect the same symmetries of the Hamiltonian the full Liouvillian spectrum displays a tower of states with properties analogous to standard quantum scars: large overlaps with N\'eel states and equally spaced gaps in the imaginary part of the eigenvalues, set by a single frequency. Whether the long-time dynamics is dominated by scars is determined by the real parts of the eigenvalues, which are set by the ratio between the two relaxation rates $\gamma_{\pm}$ describing spin flips down-to-up or up-to-down respectively. For asymmetric dissipation ($\gamma_+ > \gamma_-$) the scarred eigenvalues have the smallest real parts: the resulting enhanced fidelity oscillations are further stabilized by staggered jump operators. In contrast, replacing the full projection operator in the dissipators with a partial one breaks the symmetries of the problem and is seen to destroy dissipative scars altogether.



[148]. [*Quantum-interference correction to conductivity in disordered electronic bands with non-uniform quantum geometry*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22500 "Quantum-interference correction to conductivity in disordered electronic bands with non-uniform quantum geometry")

Yip Chun Wong, Asimpunya Mitra, Yong Baek Kim

arXiv:2610.22500v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Weak localization in two-dimensional disordered electron systems originates from constructive interference between time-reversed scattering paths, giving rise to a logarithmically divergent quantum-interference correction to the dc conductivity. This behavior is generally suppressed when time-reversal symmetry (TRS) is broken. In this study, using a band-projected framework motivated by spin- and valley-polarized rhombohedral $N$-layer graphene (RNG), we show that nonuniform quantum geometry can qualitatively modify quantum-interference corrections to dc transport. In particular, continuously tuning the quantum geometry can change the sign of the conductivity correction, corresponding to a crossover between localization and antilocalization tendencies. Remarkably, at special values of the parameter controlling the quantum geometry, weak-localization (WL) or weak-antilocalization (WAL) can re-emerge despite broken microscopic TRS. At these special points, the orbital structure of the Bloch states gives rise to an emergent antiunitary symmetry that protects a gapless Cooperon mode and restores the logarithmically divergent quantum-interference correction characteristic of WL or WAL. Our results establish nonuniform quantum geometry as a mechanism for controlling quantum interference and electron localization in multiorbital electronic systems.



[149]. [*Colour-Field Based Particle Categorization for Residual Stress Detection and Reduction in Solid SPH Simulations*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24210 "Colour-Field Based Particle Categorization for Residual Stress Detection and Reduction in Solid SPH Simulations")

Gizem Kayar

arXiv:2610.24210v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Residual stress remains in an object even in the absence of external forces or thermal pressure, which, in turn, may cause significant plastic deformations. In case the residual stress creates unwanted effects on the material and so is undesirable, an efficient solution is necessary to track and eliminate this stress. Smoothed Particle Hydrodynamics has been extensively used in solid mechanics simulations and the inherent colour-field generation approach is a promising tracker for the residual stress. In this paper, we propose a way to use the colour-field approach for eliminating the residual stress and prevent the undesirable premature failure of solid objects.



[150]. [*Development of microwave multiplexed readout for athermal phonon TES-based detectors*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24300 "Development of microwave multiplexed readout for athermal phonon TES-based detectors")

Tran Tsan (Physics Division, Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory, Berkeley, CA, USA), Douglas A. Bennett (National Institute of Standards and Technology, Boulder, CO, USA), Daniel T. Becker (Department of Physics, University of Colorado, Boulder, CO, USA), Rebecca Carney (Engineering Division, Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory, Berkeley, CA, USA), Johnathon D. Gard (Department of Physics, University of Colorado, Boulder, CO, USA), John A. B. Mates (National Institute of Standards and Technology, Boulder, CO, USA), Joel N. Ullom (National Institute of Standards and Technology, Boulder, CO, USA, Department of Physics, University of Colorado, Boulder, CO, USA), Michael R. Williams (Physics Division, Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory, Berkeley, CA, USA), John C. Groh (Physics Division, Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory, Berkeley, CA, USA)

arXiv:2610.24300v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Continued advancements in sub-eV calorimetric measurements are being enabled through the athermal phonon detection channel, with applications spanning a diverse array of fields, including low-mass dark matter searches, coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering, and quantum information science. While Transition Edge Sensors (TESs) have achieved the lowest thresholds to date, they face significant scalability bottlenecks due to the inherent constraints of conventional SQUID readout architectures. To address this limitation, we present an update on R&amp;D to develop practical, highly multiplexed, microwave readout for athermal phonon TESs, which can enable a dramatic increase in both sensor count and target mass, with a focus on sub-GeV dark matter searches as a target application. This work leverages advances from highly scaled mm-wave, x-ray, and gamma-ray TES systems, adapting them to the unique needs of low-threshold rare event measurements. We quantify the basic readout functionality and noise level in our dilution refrigerator testbed and demonstrate operation of a variety of TES devices with saturation powers significantly lower than mm-wave and x-ray devices. We demonstrate proof-of-concept multiplexed readout of background event pulses, bound the readout's contribution to the parasitic TES background load to <1 fW, and discuss a cryogenic inline RF filtering scheme. These results demonstrate practical solutions to several of the key challenges that must be solved before a next-generation athermal phonon detector at scale is realized.



[151]. [*CME-SpectrumBench: Can LLMs Analyze Condensed Matter Spectral Data?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24304 "CME-SpectrumBench: Can LLMs Analyze Condensed Matter Spectral Data?")

Jin Gene Wong, Anjney Midha, Joseph Mathew Tennyson, Wei-Lin Chiang, Ion Stoica, Zhi-Xun Shen

arXiv:2610.24304v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) are presently well assessed by expert-level mathematics and physics benchmarks which focus on theoretical, symbolic reasoning. However, we lack experimental benchmarks that evaluate data analysis in frontier research problems, a capability that future domain-specific foundation models must have. To this end, we present CME-SpectrumBench, an expert-designed benchmark consisting of 190 questions that measures how reliably frontier LLMs analyze research-level spectral scientific data in condensed matter experiments (CME). Using simulated CME spectral data, we prompt models to identify structures from 2D intensity arrays in the face of noise and limited instrumental resolution, a test of dense context reasoning. Models are scored by how accurately their numerical answers approach the ground truth. Under direct query, scores generally pale in comparison to handwritten code due to clear failure modes: models can find simple features but struggle to trace patterns or compute derived quantities, and performance typically degrades with increased data resolution and noise/convolution levels. LLM performance becomes comparable to handwritten code by allowing tool use (access to a Python interpreter) with/without ReAct prompting, in effect mapping questions onto agentic code generation problems. Our findings show that frontier models can serve as research assistants by interfacing with data through code, but still face difficulties processing information directly from large datasets - a key barrier future foundation models must overcome. The code and data are publicly available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/jingene/CMESpectrumBench.



[152]. [*Power-Law Correlations Without Apparent Temperature-Tuned Criticality in Fixed-Tree Hierarchical Languages*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24334 "Power-Law Correlations Without Apparent Temperature-Tuned Criticality in Fixed-Tree Hierarchical Languages")

Marcel K\"uhn, Bernd Rosenow

arXiv:2610.24334v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Sampling temperature can substantially reshape autoregressive generation, but it is unclear when temperature changes in long-range correlations reflect a genuine phase transition rather than a smooth change in statistics. We study this question in a controlled analogue of temperature sampling: a finite-depth probabilistic context-free grammar on a fixed hierarchical tree, where exact next-token conditionals are computed by belief propagation and then rescaled by a softmax temperature before autoregressive sampling. We measure two-point mutual information between tokens as a function of separation. Across the tested temperatures and grammar ensembles, mutual information remains well described by a power-law envelope. Temperature changes the correlation magnitude and fitted exponent, but within the accessible system sizes we find no long-distance plateau characteristic of an ordered phase and no clear crossover to short-range exponential decay. An approximate mapping to an Ising model on a tree explains how exponential decay in tree distance becomes a power law in token distance, allowing power laws to persist without tuning to a special decoding temperature. This controlled baseline clarifies which temperature effects can arise from hierarchical structure alone, before adding training, approximation error, or neural architecture.



[153]. [*Certifying Nonlocal Spin Quantum Entanglement from Local Tunneling Spectra*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24440 "Certifying Nonlocal Spin Quantum Entanglement from Local Tunneling Spectra")

Payman Kazemikhah, Ruolun Zhang, Xiangyu Luo, Byunghoon Kim, Yu-Peng Zhu, Md. Shafayat Hossain, M. Zahid Hasan

arXiv:2610.24440v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Quantum entanglement is intrinsically nonlocal, whereas tunneling spectroscopy measures one site at a time. We show that this apparent mismatch can be overcome: local tunneling spectra can certify spin entanglement without coherent control or absolute intensity calibration. An exact spin sum rule turns the high-bias conductance into an internal normalization, allowing the inelastic spin-flip weight to place a lower bound on the local quantum Fisher information. Total-spin conservation then fixes the otherwise inaccessible intersite fluctuation, converting two local spectra into a bound on a collective entanglement witness. We incorporate coupling to the substrate through a spectroscopic bound on spin leakage, while a hierarchy of thresholds distinguishes entanglement between the spins from entanglement with their environment. Including instrumental broadening and noise, the resulting certificate holds at a stated confidence level. These results establish a route for extracting nonlocal quantum information from equilibrium local spectroscopy.



[154]. [*Concatenated continuous-drive protection of exchange-based spin-qubit gates*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24582 "Concatenated continuous-drive protection of exchange-based spin-qubit gates")

Takuma Kuno, Takeru Utsugi, Andrew J. Ramsay, Hideo Arimoto, Tetsuo Kodera, Hiroyuki Mizuno

arXiv:2610.24582v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Exchange-based two-qubit gates in semiconductor spin qubits are sensitive to low-frequency detuning noise and control errors. Here, we extend concatenated continuous driving (CCD), implemented by phase modulation of a global microwave drive, to exchange-based SWAP and $\sqrt{\mathrm{SWAP}}$ operations between double-dressed spin qubits and investigate its effectiveness for protecting two-qubit gates. We find that CCD retains strong protection against quasistatic common-mode detuning errors while substantially improving robustness against Rabi-frequency errors and qubit-to-qubit Rabi-frequency mismatch compared with conventional continuous dressing. By contrast, the gate fidelity is impacted by differential detuning errors. By modeling partially correlated quasistatic detuning noise, we further show that the noise-averaged gate fidelity depends strongly on the spatial correlation of the qubit-frequency fluctuations, with positive correlations enhancing CCD protection by suppressing the differential-noise component. These results identify the symmetry and spatial correlation of qubit-frequency noise as key factors governing continuous-drive protection in coupled spin-qubit systems.



[155]. [*Boosting fluorescence and polarization with nanophotonics for single-molecule chiral sensing*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24746 "Boosting fluorescence and polarization with nanophotonics for single-molecule chiral sensing")

Ershad Mohammadi, T. V. Raziman, Alberto G. Curto

arXiv:2610.24746v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Detecting chirality is essential in chemistry and biomedicine. Circular dichroism identifies chiral molecules through their differential absorption of circularly polarized light. However, its sensitivity is limited by vanishing absorption at low concentrations. Fluorescence-based methods have already enabled other optical techniques to reach the single-molecule limit and could, in principle, also extend chiral detection to reach this sensitivity regime. They are marred, however, by the combination of weak fluorescence signals and low polarization contrast. To overcome these limitations, we leverage high-index dielectric nanophotonics to simultaneously enhance both fluorescence and polarization for chiral sensing. We propose a reciprocity-based analytical framework for a chiral dipolar emitter coupled to a nanoantenna and reveal the different contributions and trade-offs governing the enhancement of circularly polarized fluorescence. Guided by this insight, we tailor achiral dielectric nanostructures to reach a 1000-fold enhancement of fluorescence intensity together with a 10-fold increase in polarization. Crucially, we show that accounting for extrinsic polarization effects in the angular emission patterns of nanophotonic structures is indispensable for reliable enantiomer discrimination. Our approach opens a realistic path for single-molecule chiral sensing.



[156]. [*Undulatory Swimming in Sand: Subsurface Locomotion of the Sandfish Lizard*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24934 "Undulatory Swimming in Sand: Subsurface Locomotion of the Sandfish Lizard")

Ryan D. Maladen, Yang Ding, Chen Li, Daniel I. Goldman

arXiv:2610.24934v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: The desert-dwelling sandfish (Scincus scincus) moves within dry sand, a material that displays solid and fluidlike behavior. High-speed x-ray imaging shows that below the surface, the lizard no longer uses limbs for propulsion but generates thrust to overcome drag by propagating an undulatory traveling wave down the body. Although viscous hydrodynamics can predict swimming speed in fluids such as water, an equivalent theory for granular drag is not available. To predict sandfish swimming speed, we developed an empirical model by measuring granular drag force on a small cylinder oriented at different angles relative to the displacement direction and summing these forces over the animal movement profile. The agreement between model and experiment implies that the noninertial swimming occurs in a frictional fluid.



[157]. [*Dynamic Hotspot Migration and Self-Induced Resonances in Ultrafast Laser-Irradiated Silicon Thin Films*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25079 "Dynamic Hotspot Migration and Self-Induced Resonances in Ultrafast Laser-Irradiated Silicon Thin Films")

George D. Tsibidis, Yi Wang, Emmanuel Stratakis

arXiv:2610.25079v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: The spatial distribution of energy in silicon thin films under femtosecond laser irradiation is demonstrated to be influenced by the interaction between carrier-induced refractive index modulation and self-induced Fabry-P\'erot resonances. We resolve three characteristic excitation regions such as interface-localized, binodal and multi-nodal regimes considering the analysis of silicon-on-insulator (SOI) configurations across thicknesses of 100, 230, and 350 nm. We report a nonlinear hotspot migration effect where the transient reduction of the refractive index stretches the internal standing wave, translating intensity antinodes toward the film volume during the pulse. Numerical results demonstrate that at higher fluences, plasma-induced extinction suppresses internal reflections, triggering a transition from resonance-mediated absorption to plasma shielding. These findings establish a physical basis for depth-resolved modification in laser nanomanufacturing, providing a dual-capacity for simultaneous surface patterning and depth-resolved subsurface modification with direct applications in laser-assisted interfacial separation and 3D integrated photonic fabrication.



[158]. [*Generative marginal learning for finite-size molecular simulation with application to stress calculation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25105 "Generative marginal learning for finite-size molecular simulation with application to stress calculation")

Cheng Yuan

arXiv:2610.25105v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We propose generative marginal learning (GML), a framework for equilibrium sampling and local observable estimation in molecular simulations. By training a conditional diffusion model on large-system simulations, GML learns the marginal distribution of a retained region, enabling configuration generation without explicitly simulating the surrounding environment or imposing periodic boundary conditions on the retained region. We derive a marginal effective potential and the associated conditional mean-force identity, which provide the basis for both denoising and force-alignment training. As an application, we estimate the spatially averaged Irving--Kirkwood stress near a vacancy defect. Numerical results demonstrate that GML substantially improves pair structure and local stress accuracy compared with small periodic molecular dynamics simulations, mitigating finite-size effects in calculating local molecular observables.



[159]. [*Emergent Sparsity: How Many Effective Variables Survive Coarse-Graining in AlphaEarth Embeddings?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25258 "Emergent Sparsity: How Many Effective Variables Survive Coarse-Graining in AlphaEarth Embeddings?")

Johannes Hirn

arXiv:2610.25258v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: AlphaEarth encodes each 10 m land pixel as a 64-component vector intended to support diverse global mapping tasks. Yet many scientific applications work at coarser spatial supports, for which fewer than 64 effective variables may suffice. We ask whether these vectors can be reorganized into ranked global nonlinear coordinates, how many suffice at a chosen reconstruction accuracy, and whether that number decreases with spatial scale. We thus describe effective dimension as a two-control surface over tolerance and spatial scale, rather than one intrinsic number. At each scale, a $\beta$-VAE scan with a common architecture produces a rank--distortion envelope, giving the smallest retained count achieved within the scanned family at each reconstruction tolerance. At 3% validation tolerance, this number falls from 24 to 7 when the scale is increased from 10 m to 78.9 km, whereas the corresponding PCA rank falls only from 46 to 38. Repeating the analysis on unit-norm targets preserves this trend.



[160]. [*Upper Bound on the Correlation Energy of a Dense Fermi Gas in the Thermodynamic Limit*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25262 "Upper Bound on the Correlation Energy of a Dense Fermi Gas in the Thermodynamic Limit")

Niels Benedikter, Sascha Lill, Marcello Porta, Benjamin Schlein

arXiv:2610.25262v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We prove that the random phase approximation provides an upper bound on the correlation energy of a three-dimensional, weakly interacting, Fermi gas in the thermodynamic limit at high density. More precisely, we consider a mean-field scaling regime where the kinetic and interaction Hamiltonian are scaled by $\rho^{-2/3}$ and $\rho^{-1}$, respectively. We use a correlated trial state constructed through a quasi-bosonic Bogoliubov transformation of collective particle--hole excitations associated to patches on the Fermi surface. Key of the proof is to control the position-space localization of the collective excitations. Combined with an iterated Duhamel expansion of the expectation values of (localized) particle number operators we obtain extensive estimates, which justify the reduction to an effective quadratic Hamiltonian and its approximate diagonalization.



[161]. [*The Anyon Atlas*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25439 "The Anyon Atlas")

Jonah Berean-Dutcher, Clay Cordova, Julio Parra-Martinez, Richard Tuddenham

arXiv:2610.25439v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We classify possible topological quantum field theories in 2+1 dimensions with up to 22 anyons in the bosonic case and up to 26 in the fermionic case, through the modular data of modular and supermodular tensor categories. We construct such data via congruence representations of $\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{Z})$ using a numerical optimization algorithm that scales to high rank. We chart the relations between the resulting theories, including anyon condensations and Chern-Simons realizations. We find examples of modular data that do not appear to arise from any Chern-Simons theory by any sequence of condensations of anyons or gaugings of ordinary global symmetries. These examples give qualitatively new topological quantum field theories, which go beyond any notion of gauge theory. For examples in this class with 11 and 12 anyon types, we solve the pentagon and hexagon equations explicitly, showing that these modular data are realized by full unitary topological quantum field theories. We also introduce https://www.AnyonAtlas.org, an online resource for exploring the data.



[162]. [*Topological Quantum Gates on FCIs with Bounded Quantum Geometry*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25472 "Topological Quantum Gates on FCIs with Bounded Quantum Geometry")

Hisham Sati, Urs Schreiber

arXiv:2610.25472v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Two-band 2D fractional Chern insulators (FCIs) have recently been shown, in principle, to admit unusual anyonic order, associated with Bloch Hopfions localized in momentum space. Here we show that bounding the integrated quantum metric along one primitive Brillouin-zone cycle may refine this topological order to $|C|$ distinct braiding observables, cyclically permuted by lattice translations, for Chern numbers $|C| > 1$. The cyclic structure entails mildly non-abelian braiding phases and otherwise matches the spectral flow exhibited by the FCI's hybrid Wannier states, with the metric bound equivalently a bound on the states' real-space spread.
  We give a classification of quantum gates in principle implementable on this topological order; among them we find the non-Clifford T-gate and the non-Clifford entangling CCZ-gate. This points to novel pathways to fault-tolerant quantum processes within the known orbital structure of FCIs, potentially informing experimental searches towards hardware for future topological quantum computers.



[163]. [*Subsystem quantum R\'enyi-Bhattacharyya distance in 2D CFTs and its holographic applications*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25685 "Subsystem quantum R\'enyi-Bhattacharyya distance in 2D CFTs and its holographic applications")

Yihao Wang, Jiaju Zhang

arXiv:2610.25685v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We develop a systematic method to compute the subsystem fidelity and R\'enyi fidelity in two-dimensional conformal field theories (CFTs) from the operator product expansion of twist operators, and apply it to the subsystem quantum Bhattacharyya distance (BD) and R\'enyi-Bhattacharyya distance (RBD). We validate the method against exact results in the free massless fermion and boson theories, and then use it to compute the BD and RBD in holographic CFTs between two heavy primary states, between two thermal states, and between a heavy primary state and a thermal state. When the two states differ in their classical, order-$c$ data, the distances are of order $c$, and the states are distinguishable already at the level of classical gravity; when the leading data coincide, the distances are of order $1/c$ at every order of the short interval expansion, so that the distinguishability becomes a perturbative quantum gravity effect. For a heavy primary state and its thermal counterpart, the leading contribution to the BD appears at order $\ell^8$ and is of order $c^0$, whereas the RBD is generically of order $c$, in line with the earlier finding that a heavy primary state and a thermal state share the same order-$c$ entanglement entropy but have different order-$c$ R\'enyi entropies in the short interval expansion.



[164]. [*The long-range Ising CFT: perturbative and nonperturbative approaches*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25940 "The long-range Ising CFT: perturbative and nonperturbative approaches")

Zechuan Zheng

arXiv:2610.25940v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: The long-range Ising model is the prototype of criticality with power-law interactions. In each dimension its interacting critical points form a one-parameter family of nonlocal conformal field theories, weakly coupled at both ends but not in between. We study this family in one, two and three dimensions from several independent directions. We extend the expansion about the generalized free field from three to eight loops and expand about the short-range end. We show that Hamiltonian truncation applies to this nonlocal theory once a truncation artifact is corrected. Where they can be compared, the resummed expansions, Hamiltonian truncation, the numerical renormalization group and Monte Carlo simulations agree on the dimension of the leading $\mathbb{Z}_2$-even scalar at the sub-percent level, as universality requires.



[165]. [*Phonon-dominated thermal conductivity of a superconducting amorphous MoSi thin film*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26136 "Phonon-dominated thermal conductivity of a superconducting amorphous MoSi thin film")

Jacopo Piantanida Chiesa, Tom Q. Post, Martin P. van Exter, Michiel J. A. de Dood

arXiv:2610.26136v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We use the differential 3{\omega} method to directly measure the temperature dependent thermal conductivity for about 90 nm Mo0.78Si0.22 thin films. The measurements are performed in a closed-cycle cryostat to access the temperature range between 50 K and 300 K. The MoSi thin films are capped with Al2O3 and probed with a Pt wire. The results show that the thermal conductivity does not satisfy the Wiedemann-Franz law. This implies that thermal transport in MoSi is not dominated by electrons but by phonon-like excitations, contrary to what is assumed in many electro-thermal models of superconducting devices. We argue that this property might be related to the weak localization visible in the resistivity as a function of temperature curves of the superconductor.



[166]. [*Quantum control as renormalization group flow*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26154 "Quantum control as renormalization group flow")

Zuo Wang, Paul M. Schindler, Modesto Orozco-Ruiz, Nguyen H. Le, Florian Mintert, Marin Bukov

arXiv:2610.26154v1 Announce Type: cross
