Abstract: The Renormalization Group (RG) establishes relations between effective descriptions of physical systems at different scales; in Hamiltonian RG, these relations induce a flow in the space of Hamiltonians. In direct correspondence, we design an RG framework for quantum control by constructing a flow in the space of time-dependent Hamiltonians. A prescribed control subspace, selected to contain only experimentally accessible terms, forms the fixed-point manifold, while the flow preserves the target unitary and monotonically reduces the Hamiltonian weight outside this subspace. Exploiting the freedom in constructing the flow, we introduce flow-equation engineering to enhance the versatility of the framework for diverse control problems, including time-periodic and open-interval smooth protocols with prescribed endpoints. We provide two disparate applications to exhibit the utility of our approach: (i) Nonperturbative Floquet engineering beyond the regime of validity of high-frequency expansions, which we demonstrate by constructing an implementable time-periodic protocol for the Haldane model; (ii) Accessible fast-forward driving from unit-fidelity counterdiabatic evolution, which we showcase for large transverse-field Ising chains away from the adiabatic regime by compressing the long-range multibody adiabatic gauge potential into the original time-dependent Ising control schedules. Our control RG framework reveals a unifying perspective on Hamiltonian engineering and unitary quantum control, and can serve as an organizing principle behind seemingly disparate nonequilibrium quantum dynamics.



[167]. [*Dynamical Batching: How Class-Cycling Training Shapes the Internal Structure of Neural Networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26156 "Dynamical Batching: How Class-Cycling Training Shapes the Internal Structure of Neural Networks")

Nicol\'as Ratier-Werbin, Samuel Lozano, Miguel Ruiz-Garc\'ia

arXiv:2610.26156v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We investigate whether cyclically varying the class composition of training batches can modify both the performance and the internal organization of neural networks. Motivated by nonequilibrium systems, in which cycling between related sub-goals can make switching between their solutions increasingly efficient, we introduce Dynamical Batching (DB), a sampling protocol that periodically emphasizes each class while leaving the loss and the optimizer unchanged, and compare it with Uniform Batching (UB), in which every mini-batch has equal class proportions. In the regime where standard training fails, DB changes the outcome: on a three-arm spiral, a capacity-limited MLP that plateaus far below perfect accuracy under UB fits the training data under DB. Tracking individual weights, we find that under DB the change between class-specialized states is carried by a smaller set of weights than under UB, and that this set is markedly more persistent from cycle to cycle, on every synthetic dataset we test and in a wider network that fits the data under both protocols. This organization is a signature of the protocol rather than a predictor of its benefit: it appears equally where DB leaves accuracy unchanged. On CIFAR-10, the same protocol modestly improves the test accuracy of a channel-narrowed ResNet-10, a standard residual architecture, and of a second capacity-limited convolutional network. These results show that how class emphasis is distributed in time can shape both the solution a network reaches and the internal structure of the network.



[168]. [*Sidewall Metrology Protocol for Superconducting Qubit Devices*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26223 "Sidewall Metrology Protocol for Superconducting Qubit Devices")

J{\o}rgen Garn{\ae}s, Henrik S. Guttesen, Astrid T. R{\o}mer, Oscar Danielsson, Emily P. Duggan, Zhenhai Sun, Asen Lyubenov Georgiev, Li Wei Chang, Morten Kjaergaard, Mark Bieler, Lukas Gr\"unhaupt, Sabbir A. Khan

arXiv:2610.26223v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Sidewall morphology is a potentially important factor influencing superconducting qubit performance, yet its quantitative characterization remains challenging due to the absence of standardized measurement protocols. In this work, we develop a scanning probe microscopy-based measurement framework for the quantitative characterization of superconducting qubit sidewalls. The framework introduces a refined root mean square lateral sidewall roughness descriptor and a new curvature-equivalent radius for quantifying vertical sidewall morphology. The descriptors and measurement procedures are applied to different components of qubit devices, forming the basis of a proposed sidewall characterization protocol. This provides a foundation for quantitative comparisons of fabrication processes across laboratories, enabling future investigations of correlations between sidewall geometry and qubit performance.



[169]. [*The resource cost of cooperative invasion*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26235 "The resource cost of cooperative invasion")

Abhishek Chowdhury

arXiv:2610.26235v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: A contact network can favor cooperation by sheltering a lineage until its descendants compete with defectors as a group. We determine the contact strengths, invasion times and measurement effort needed to realize this advantage at fixed population size. Individuals reproduce according to averaged cooperation payoffs, and offspring replace neighbors through contacts of equal weight in both directions. Contact contrast is the ratio of strongest to weakest contact. Under weak selection on a six-site path, the least contrast needed to favor a single cooperator over the reverse invasion by a defector grows as $656\epsilon^{-2}$ for uniformly placed mutants and $4050\epsilon^{-3}$ for mutations in newborn offspring, as the benefit-cost ratio $b/c=1+\epsilon$ approaches one. The extra power arises because rare replacement protects a site while suppressing mutation arrival there. Global bounds force two weakly coupled groups and exact mirror symmetry of the least-contrast contacts for sufficiently small $\epsilon$. Under uniform introduction, additional contrast shortens the leading mean time to extinction or fixation in the absence of selection, from $12/\epsilon$ toward $6/\epsilon$ reproduction attempts, and eventually breaks the mirror symmetry of the fastest design. Estimating the uniform-introduction advantage from fixation outcomes of independent completed invasions defines a third optimum. Within the six-site family with mirror-symmetric, strongly separated contacts, we optimize contacts and selection strength with reproduction weighted linearly or exponentially by payoff. At fixed relative precision, with contrast a fixed factor above its minimum, the leading summed trial duration grows as $\epsilon^{-3}$. Efficient measurement accepts a smaller advantage in exchange for shorter trials. The cheapest, fastest and most efficiently measured networks therefore differ.



[170]. [*Universal Quantum Criticality from a Gaussian Variational Manifold*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26246 "Universal Quantum Criticality from a Gaussian Variational Manifold")

Reyhaneh Khasseh, Fabian Ballar Trigueros, Markus Heyl, M. A. Rajabpour

arXiv:2610.26246v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Universal behavior at continuous phase transitions is encoded in long-wavelength fixed-point theories controlled by dimensionality and symmetries. It is a central question for which systems this universal behavior and the underlying long-wavelength description can be captured by a noninteracting theory allowing for analytical and accurate predictions. In this work we provide a systematic procedure for obtaining the best noninteracting fermionic description of a quantum phase transition through fermionic Gaussian states. We demonstrate the performance of our procedure for the interacting long-range transverse-field Ising chain. This formulation reduces the problem to a self-consistent Euler equation that yields a critical line in close agreement with independent numerical benchmarks, while its singular momentum structure provides analytic predictions for continuously varying critical properties. Yet exact diagonalization shows that Gaussian-state fidelity to the exact ground state is poorest, and non-Gaussianity strongest, near the transition. Thus, accurate predictions for critical properties can emerge from the Gaussian variational description even where its microscopic wave-function approximation is weakest.



[171]. [*Optical vortex bullets with large angular momenta*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26289 "Optical vortex bullets with large angular momenta")

Jos\'{e} R. Salgueiro, Carlos F. S\'{a}nchez, Boris A. Malomed, \'{A}ngel Paredes, Humberto Michinel

arXiv:2610.26289v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We investigate three-dimensional spatiotemporal optical vortex solitons (light bullets with a toroidal shape) carrying higher-order orbital angular momentum in Kerr media with anomalous group-velocity dispersion and a transversely localized Gaussian profile of the linear refractive index. Stationary vortex bullets with topological charges (winding numbers) $1\leq \ell \leq 4$ are obtained as numerical solutions of the three-dimensional nonlinear Schr\"{o}dinger equation, and their existence, structure and stability are systematically analyzed. In terms of atomic Bose-Einstein condensates, these solutions represent matter-wave solitons with embedded vorticity. We examine the dependence of the solution families on the propagation constant, the depth and width of the guiding profile, and $\ell $. It is found that the increase of $\ell $ requires one to use a stronger or wider waveguiding potential to sustain localized states satisfying the Vakhitov-Kolokolov (VK) stability criterion. VK-complying and non-complying branches of vortex-soliton families are separated by a degenerate Townes branch. Direct simulations reveal that, although the guiding profile substantially suppresses the collapse and extends the propagation distance of vortex bullets, their robustness is ultimately limited by azimuthal instabilities, whose growth is enhanced for values of $\ell $. These results demonstrate that engineered refractive-index landscapes significantly enlarge the stability domain of spatiotemporal vortex bullets and provide guidelines for supporting optical bullets carrying large orbital angular momenta.



[172]. [*Fate of Arnold tongues and canards in Mobius systems with noise*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26354 "Fate of Arnold tongues and canards in Mobius systems with noise")

Artem Alexandrov

arXiv:2610.26354v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We consider a class of noisy dynamical systems on the two-dimensional torus whose phase evolution is governed by Mobius transformations. In the deterministic limit, these systems favor phase-locking that is described by the band-gap structure of an associated Schrodinger equation with a periodic potential. We use the Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin method to characterize this structure in the slow-fast limit. Then we show that large-deviation theory describes fluctuations of the finite-time rotation number, whereas weak-noise escape actions determine the exponential scale of rare phase slips. These estimates provide quantitative criteria for metastable phase-locking and melting of Arnold tongues over a prescribed observation time. We derive sufficient conditions for stochastic trajectories to follow prescribed canard segments. With controlled entrance errors, exponential amplification of fluctuations along repelling branches leads to an exponentially small sufficient noise scale for canard persistence in the slow-forcing limit.



[173]. [*Ultrafast Proton-Induced Collective Dynamics Regulated by Water-Mediated Hydrogen-Bond Networks in Liquids*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26387 "Ultrafast Proton-Induced Collective Dynamics Regulated by Water-Mediated Hydrogen-Bond Networks in Liquids")

Xiaokun Liu, Bozhu Chen, Yizhu Zhang, Jiyu Xu, Meng Li, Yanjun Gao, Ji Qi, Yuqian Yang, Wennan Wang, Sheng Meng, Yuhai Jiang

arXiv:2610.26387v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Photoinduced proton dynamics in liquids couple molecular reactions to nonequilibrium charge transport, yet the role of alkyl C-H sites as proton sources remains poorly understood. Combining optical-pump terahertz-probe spectroscopy with real-time time-dependent density-functional-theory simulations, we investigate ultrafast proton dynamics following strong-field ionization of liquid ethanol and hydroxyl-free ethyl acetate. Isotope-substitution and control experiments support the identification of alkyl C-H groups as the proton source. The released protons induce quasi-free-electron localization on a timescale of hundreds of femtoseconds, manifested as a characteristic V-shaped transient in terahertz absorption. In ethanol-water mixtures, trace water enhances hydrogen-bond-network connectivity and facilitates proton transfer to ethanol O-H sites, suppressing the transient absorption dip. These findings identify photoactivated C-H sites as proton sources in liquids and reveal how hydrogen-bond networks regulate the ensuing proton and electron dynamics, linking local bond cleavage to the ultrafast charge response of the surrounding liquid.



[174]. [*Systemic collapse emergence from climate-induced perturbations in transportation networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26428 "Systemic collapse emergence from climate-induced perturbations in transportation networks")

Mauro Faccin, Tomas Scagliarini, Manlio De Domenico

arXiv:2610.26428v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: The interaction between human infrastructures and external events results in loss of functionality or even complete system disruption through a snowball effect of cascading-failures. Their complex interplay challenges their prevention, affecting the human population that rely on their functionality. Understanding how these networks respond to external perturbations is crucial for predicting and mitigating the impacts of disruptive events. In particular, meteorological events, while not preventable, can have significant impacts on human infrastructure. Here, we propose a model coupling the dynamics of transportation systems to the dynamics of external events considered as fields, to capture at the same time the critical behavior of the former and the level of influence of the latter. The impact of rainfalls on real-world railway networks provides a compelling case study, showcasing local endemic and system-wide disruptions around a critical value of the rainfall strength. Remarkably, localized perturbations on the wider network reveals the local weaknesses of the global infrastructure, and we assess them in terms of system perturbability and corresponding societal risk. Our framework opens the door to the quantitative analysis of criticalities in essential human infrastructure imputable to vulnerabilities that are set to intensify due to climate change.



[175]. [*End-to-End Optimization of Partial Fault Tolerance in Neutral-Atom Arrays*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26471 "End-to-End Optimization of Partial Fault Tolerance in Neutral-Atom Arrays")

Stefano Veroni, Aydin Deger, Andrew J. Daley

arXiv:2610.26471v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Fault-tolerant (FT) quantum computation offers a pathway to quantum advantage, but its realization is hindered by its significant overhead. The partially FT "STAR" architecture reduces the cost of universal computation at the price of relaxing FT requirements for non-Clifford operations, increasing exposure to hardware noise. Therefore, maximizing its performance and applicability demands careful co-design with the underlying hardware. In this work we investigate the co-design of the STAR architecture with neutral-atom platforms, connecting the underlying atomic physics to logical error modeling and application-level performance. At the physical level, we identify a favorable noise bias in $^{171}\mathrm{Yb}$ Rydberg gates, and show how it can be enhanced through error-selective pulse optimization. At the logical level, we identify an intrinsic error suppression mechanism, yielding vanishing logical coherent noise in leading order. Detailed circuit-level simulations of surface code implementations show more than an order of magnitude reduction in logical error relative to previous estimates. As a concrete target application, we focus on the Fermi-Hubbard model with periodic boundaries, for which we design a simulation circuit particularly suited to early implementations. We quantify the resource tradeoff of partially and fully FT approaches to gate synthesis, with the former showing approximately an order of magnitude reduction in state-injection depth, and an even larger reduction in Clifford resources. Comparing with classical and fully FT approaches, we identify regimes where partial fault tolerance provides both lower overheads and lower error rates. In our estimates, this is accessible with moderate error rates $p\sim 10^{-3}$ and a few thousand qubits, underlining that for these types of problems, partial FT approaches provide a compelling pathway to early quantum advantage.



[176]. [*Coupling Evaporation, Chemical Reactions, and Crystallization: A Population-Balance Framework Applied to Two-Step Perovskite Crystallization*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26591 "Coupling Evaporation, Chemical Reactions, and Crystallization: A Population-Balance Framework Applied to Two-Step Perovskite Crystallization")

K. Segadlo, O. J. J. Ronsin, J. Harting

arXiv:2610.26591v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: A limited understanding of how evaporation couples to crystallization pathways hampers control over the formation of solution-processed perovskite photoactive layers. Identifying these mechanisms-and learning how to select and tune them-is essential for achieving the desired crystal morphology and, ultimately, improving solar-cell performance and stability. To investigate crystallization pathways in evaporating thin films, we develop a simple and general zero-dimensional population-balance model that couples evaporation, chemical reactions, and crystallization. We use the model to explore a range of parameters for a solvent-solute system that follows a two-step crystallization pathway: solvent evaporation first induces formation of a crystalline precursor, which subsequently recrystallizes into the final phase. The model predicts the evolution of the film composition during drying and provides quantitative LaMer curves. Based on these results, we introduce a vocabulary of "supply limitations" and "rate limitations" that enables the interpretation of crystallization kinetics in terms of the governing process conditions.



[177]. [*Non-Markovian exceptional points and information backflow: a Heisenberg reaction-coordinate approach*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26620 "Non-Markovian exceptional points and information backflow: a Heisenberg reaction-coordinate approach")

Daniel Holst, Gianmichele Blasi, Shishir Khandelwal

arXiv:2610.26620v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: The primary pathway to the investigation of exceptional points (EPs) in open quantum systems has been the use of Markovian master equations. However, recently it has been realised that they can arise in settings that show non-Markovian behaviour. The main approach in this direction has been the use of pseudomodes, which allow the influence of a structured environment to be replaced by new damped auxiliary modes. In this work, we use a Heisenberg-equation approach which relies on the reaction-coordinate formalism to obtain exact analytical dynamics. First, we show how our method considerably simplifies the analysis compared to the use of master equations. Second, we utilise it to discuss non-Markovian revivals in systems with second and higher-order EPs, showing that the notion that EPs demarcate the onset of revivals is not generally true. We then utilise our results to discuss the dynamical effects of higher-order non-Markovian EPs, deriving bounds on the number of revivals and investigating enhanced asymptotic relaxation near higher-order EPs. Finally, we investigate non-Markovian EPs with engineered reservoirs and show how they can be utilised to control the rate of irreversible information loss.



[178]. [*Average lower bound on magnetic double-well tunneling with asymptotically large well separation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26637 "Average lower bound on magnetic double-well tunneling with asymptotically large well separation")

Charles L. Fefferman, Say-Yeon Kwon, Jacob Shapiro, Michael I. Weinstein

arXiv:2610.26637v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We study tunneling between two identical, smooth, compactly supported potential wells in a constant perpendicular magnetic field, with the coupling and magnetic field fixed and the well separation tending to infinity. For a sufficiently small positive magnetic field, we prove lower bounds on the hopping coefficient and on the splitting of the two lowest eigenvalues outside a set of separations of density zero. If the well centers are separated by $2d_1$ and the magnetic field strength is $b\lambda$, the lower bounds have the form $e^{-b\lambda d_1^2-Cd_1^{1+\delta}}$ for every fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$. No radial or reflection symmetry of the single-well potential is assumed. The proof combines the explicit Landau resolvent kernel with a complex analytic estimate for logarithmic averages of the hopping coefficient.



[179]. [*Challenging Atomistic Foundation Models on Disordered Carbons*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26701 "Challenging Atomistic Foundation Models on Disordered Carbons")

Joseph Hart, Samuel G. H. Brookes, Fabian L. Thiemann, Christoph Schran

arXiv:2610.26701v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Foundation machine-learned interatomic potentials (MLIPs) promise broad transferability, yet their out-of-domain reliability is hard to establish. Here, we use pure and O/H-containing disordered carbon spanning multiple phases, competing hybridisations and porous frameworks to stress-test six popular foundation MLIPs. We first screen all six on density functional theory (DFT) energy and force errors and binding curves, then subject four to melt-quench-anneal molecular dynamics and evaluate the resulting structural observables. Binding curves and O/H-containing trajectories reveal unphysical extrapolation and dynamical failures that low DFT energy and force errors fail to reveal. Of the models tested, MACE-MH-1 is the most consistently robust, with low DFT errors and stable MD. Despite differing architectures, OMAT-based models share structural biases, highlighting the importance of training coverage. All four models generate structures of nanoporous carbon that reproduce the principal experimental and specialised carbon potential signatures. Incorporating O/H leaves short-range order largely unchanged but shifts pore-size distributions towards larger pores. These results emphasise the need for application-specific validation and extend molecular dynamics modelling of nanoporous carbon towards experimentally realistic structures.



[180]. [*A boundary integral method for computing electron repulsion integrals in heterogeneous dielectric environments*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26745 "A boundary integral method for computing electron repulsion integrals in heterogeneous dielectric environments")

Xuanzhao Gao, Malte R\"osner, Jason Kaye, Alex Barnett

arXiv:2610.26745v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We present a boundary integral method for evaluating electron repulsion integrals (ERIs) in dielectric environments of piecewise constant permittivity. This generalizes conventional bare-Coulomb ERIs by incorporating screening from structured dielectric surroundings, such as the substrates and/or encapsulating layers present in modern two-dimensional-material devices. We formulate the problem as a second-kind boundary integral equation for the induced surface charge on the dielectric interfaces, and discretize it with a Nystr\"om method on adaptively refined panels. The incident potential generated by each smooth orbital source density is evaluated by a Fourier truncated-kernel method in or near to its source box, and by fast multipole method (FMM) accelerated quadrature farther away. The linear system is solved by a Krylov iteration also accelerated by the FMM. We focus on cuboidal device geometries, and retain efficiency at high aspect ratios via close-to-touching panel quadratures. We further develop a distributed pipeline that computes the full ERI tensor, comprising thousands of orbital pairs, embedded in a single dielectric geometry. We demonstrate the method on an effective three-layer graphene slab over a Si/SiO$_2$ heterojunction, focusing on the central graphene layer represented by $198$ $p_z$-like orbitals, retaining the $2613^2$ non-negligible entries of the $198^4$-sized ERI tensor. The model is resolved on $1.3$ million surface unknowns, achieving a relative error of $0.004\%$, at a cost of $22$ node-hours.



[181]. [*Physics-Constrained Modeling and Inference of the Equation of State for Dissociative Materials*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26791 "Physics-Constrained Modeling and Inference of the Equation of State for Dissociative Materials")

Jolypich Pek, Ben Seiyon Lee, Jason Bernstein, Philip Myint

arXiv:2610.26791v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Equations of state (EOSs) describe how a material's internal energy and pressure change with temperature and density. They are inputs to hydrodynamic simulations in materials science and planetary science. We model the ion-thermal free-energy term for dissociative materials, where molecular bonds break apart to form new species under extreme conditions. This term is governed by two latent physical processes, a molar-mass surface and a Debye-temperature curve, neither of which is observed directly. Instead, both must be recovered from internal energy and pressure, which are derivatives of the free energy, subject to thermodynamic constraints. Existing EOS tables are typically built through material-specific hand-tuning, a process that is tedious, does not scale across materials or conditions, and offers no guarantee of thermodynamic consistency. We propose a partitioned statistical framework that infers molar mass and Debye temperature directly from EOS data, guaranteeing thermodynamic consistency by construction. Local molar-mass behavior is captured with polynomial basis functions across domain partitions, while the Debye temperature is modeled with an I-spline basis. In simulations and a carbon dioxide case study, the method accurately recovers the underlying physical quantities, improves prediction of held-out energy and pressure data, and provides uncertainty quantification while scaling with EOS table size.



[182]. [*Field theory guides Monte Carlo in the one-dimensional long-range Ising model*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26838 "Field theory guides Monte Carlo in the one-dimensional long-range Ising model")

Ant\'onio Antunes, Jos\'e Matos

arXiv:2610.26838v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: The statistical long-range Ising (LRI) model in one dimension provides a family of some of the simplest non-exactly solvable critical points. These critical points are described by long-range conformal field theories (CFTs) that are well understood analytically with perturbative expansions and some exact results. Guided by this, we revisit the Monte Carlo (MC) approach to the corresponding lattice model which has many favorable features compared to other systems studied with MC methods. In particular, with very modest computational cost, we are able to estimate scaling dimensions of leading and subleading operators as well as the associated three-point functions, and even certain four-point functions. These results help highlight the fact that 1d LRI CFTs are a phenomenal playground to more generally test non-perturbative methods and ideas for strongly coupled CFTs.



[183]. [*Gate-Switchable Josephson Diode Effect in Graphene via Magnetochiral Anisotropy*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.02692 "Gate-Switchable Josephson Diode Effect in Graphene via Magnetochiral Anisotropy")

Chuan-Shuai Huang, Xing He, Ruihao Zhang

arXiv:2312.02692v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The Josephson diode effect originating from magnetochiral anisotropy has thus far been realized primarily in conventional two-dimensional electron gases. Here, we propose a graphene-based Josephson junction as a broadly gate-tunable platform for nonreciprocal supercurrent transport. We demonstrate that the nonreciprocal critical current not only reverses its sign when graphene is switched from $n$- to $p$-type doping via electrostatic gating, but its efficiency can also be continuously tuned from zero up to $\sim 40\%$. This gate-controlled polarity switching and efficiency modulation---unique to the chiral nature of Dirac fermions in graphene---has no counterpart in conventional semiconductor systems, positioning the graphene Josephson diode as a highly versatile component for advanced superconducting circuits.



[184]. [*Absorbing state transitions with long-range annihilation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03280 "Absorbing state transitions with long-range annihilation")

Nicholas O'Dea, Sayak Bhattacharjee, Sarang Gopalakrishnan, Vedika Khemani

arXiv:2409.03280v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We introduce a family of classical stochastic processes describing diffusive particles undergoing branching and long-range annihilation in the presence of a parity constraint. The probability for a pair-annihilation event decays as a power-law in the distance between particles, with a tunable exponent. Such long-range processes arise naturally in various classical settings, and increasingly play a role in the study of quantum dynamics: for example, quantum state preparation or error correction processes aim to prepare ordered ground states, which requires removing point-like excitations in pairs via non-local feedback operations conditioned on a global set of measurement outcomes. We analytically and numerically describe features of absorbing phases and phase transitions in this family of classical models as pairwise annihilation is performed at larger and larger distances. Notably, we find that the two canonical absorbing-state universality classes -- directed-percolation and parity-conserving -- are endpoints of a new line of universality classes with continuously varying critical exponents. These new classes follow from an interplay of long-range interactions and symmetries: decreasing the long-range exponent effectively weakens the local effects of global symmetry constraints. This competition significantly broadens known non-equilibrium phases and phase transitions.



[185]. [*Coherence and pairing enhancement in a trilayer cuprate with layer-selective charge order*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01448 "Coherence and pairing enhancement in a trilayer cuprate with layer-selective charge order")

S. Smit, M. Bluschke, P. Moen, N. Heinsdorf, E. Zavatti, G. Bellomia, S. Giuli, S. K. Y. Dufresne, C. T. Suen, V. Zimmermann, C. Au-Yeung, S. Zhdanovich, J. I. Dadap, P. Dosanjh, M. Zonno, S. Gorovikov, H. Lee, C-T. Kuo, J-S. Lee, D. Song, S. Ishida, H. Eisaki, B. Keimer, M. Michiardi, I. S. Elfimov, G. Levy, D. J. Jones, M. Capone, A. Damascelli

arXiv:2506.01448v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Trilayer cuprates hold the record for the highest superconducting critical temperatures ($T_{\text{c}}$), yet the underlying mechanism remains elusive. Using time- and angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (tr-ARPES), we uncover a striking interplay between charge order, superconducting gap magnitude, and quasiparticle coherence in Bi$_2$Sr$_2$Ca$_2$Cu$_3$O$_{10+\delta}$ (Bi2223). We present ARPES-based evidence of charge order on the inner CuO$_2$ plane -- as confirmed via resonant x-ray scattering (RXS) -- and show that the same inner plane hosts a large superconducting gap matching its doping, firmly establishing it as underdoped. Despite its underdoped nature, the inner plane also exhibits an exceptional degree of quasiparticle coherence; suppressing charge-order fluctuations further enhances this, making it comparable to that of the overdoped outer planes at elevated electronic temperatures. At the same time, on the overdoped outer planes we find larger superconducting gaps than what is seen on comparably doped single and bilayer cuprates, indicating an unexpected and simultaneous enhancement of both coherence and pairing interactions in Bi2223. These findings, supported by complementary single- and three-layer Hubbard model calculations, reveal a unique interlayer mechanism in which both pairing strength and phase coherence are optimized when interfacing planes with distinct hole concentrations, providing new microscopic insight into the record $T_{\text{c}}$ of Bi2223.



[186]. [*Surface-extended Andreev bound states emerging from intrinsic surface superconductivity of PtBi$_2$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13843 "Surface-extended Andreev bound states emerging from intrinsic surface superconductivity of PtBi$_2$")

Xiaochun Huang, Lingxiao Zhao, Sebastian Schimmel, Julia Besproswanny, Luminita Harnagea, Patrick H\"artl, Christian Hess, Bernd B\"uchner, Matthias Bode

arXiv:2507.13843v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Intrinsic topological superconductivity remains one of the central open questions in condensed-matter physics. The three-dimensional Weyl semimetal PtBi$_2$ has recently been shown by angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy to host a superconducting gap that opens exclusively on its Fermi-arc surface states and exhibits a nodal structure, making it a promising platform for exploring unconventional and potentially topological superconductivity. However, despite these momentum-resolved insights, the real-space spectroscopic signatures of unconventional superconductivity in PtBi$_2$ remain unclear. Here, using scanning tunneling microscopy and spectroscopy, we reveal a sizable and spatially homogeneous gap ($\Delta > 10\,\mathrm{meV}$) on atomically pristine terraces of PtBi$_2$. Within this gap, we detect surface-extended in-gap states exhibiting pronounced atomic-scale spatial modulation. Analysis of the tunneling spectra indicates that these states are most consistently interpreted as Andreev bound states within a superconducting gap. Further phenomenological modeling shows that their characteristic spectral features are naturally captured by a chiral pairing scenario. Our results establish a direct real-space connection between the superconducting gap and surface-extended quasiparticle states in PtBi$_2$, and provide new insight into the nature of its unconventional surface superconductivity. These findings highlight PtBi$_2$ as a versatile platform for exploring exotic quasiparticle excitations and potential Majorana physics.



[187]. [*Quantum Geometric Injection and Shift Optical Forces Drive Coherent Phonons*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17814 "Quantum Geometric Injection and Shift Optical Forces Drive Coherent Phonons")

J. Luke Pimlott, Habib Rostami

arXiv:2507.17814v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We develop a gauge-invariant quantum-geometric theory of light-induced Raman forces driving coherent lattice dynamics in solids. We show that the resonant interband response separates into injection and shift forces, providing the lattice analogue of injection and shift photocurrents. Crucially, the electronic quantum metric and Berry curvature directly govern these forces, weighted by distinct electron--phonon quantities. The injection force is controlled by the quantum geometric tensor and differences between band-diagonal electron--phonon couplings, producing the resonant displacive response. The shift force is a resonant impulsive response governed by quantum geometry and a phononic shift vector arising from interband electron--phonon coherence. We derive polarization selection rules, symmetry constraints, and temporal signatures. Applied to interlayer shear phonons in a bilayer Haldane model, the theory predicts tunability with optical frequency, topology, electronic temperature, and current-induced symmetry breaking. These results establish a quantum-geometric framework for coherent lattice dynamics and identify coherent phonons as experimentally accessible signatures of electronic quantum geometry in quantum materials.



[188]. [*Kolmogorov-type non-thermal fixed points and beyond of far-from-equilibrium dilute system: ultra-cold Fermi gas*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18821 "Kolmogorov-type non-thermal fixed points and beyond of far-from-equilibrium dilute system: ultra-cold Fermi gas")

Chun-Wei Su

arXiv:2508.18821v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The far-from-equilibrium dynamics driven by the scattering from next-to-leading-order (NLO) corrections in the quantum field theory has stationary solutions for the particle distribution characterized as the Kolmogorov-type non-thermal fixed points corresponding to scale-invariant particle/energy flux. The dynamics of the spatially homogeneous dilute ultra-cold Fermi gas is investigated, and its kinetic equation confirms the Kolmogorov-type non-thermal fixed points in the perturbation theory by the quasi-particle assumption, in contrast to the wave turbulence of the ultra-cold Bose gas. In addition, other stationary states are discussed when the the vertex correction becomes significant in the kinetic equation. It is found that conditions for the scale-invariant flux in this case no longer hold when the quasi-particle assumption is applied.



[189]. [*Numerical and theoretical studies of the Anderson transition in the chiral symmetry classes with weak topology*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16555 "Numerical and theoretical studies of the Anderson transition in the chiral symmetry classes with weak topology")

Shiyin Kuang, Tong Wang, Zhenyu Xiao, Pengwei Zhao, Ryuichi Shindou

arXiv:2509.16555v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Combining lattice-model simulations with a renormalization group (RG) analysis of effective theories, we investigate the nature of the Anderson transition in chiral symmetry classes with one-dimensional (1D) weak topology. In the simulation study, we extend previous transfer matrix analyses to the chiral symplectic class, and study numerical Lyapunov exponents via a finite-size scaling (FSS) analysis that assumes spatially isotropic scaling. The analysis shows that, as in the other two chiral symmetry classes, the weak topology induces an intermediate quasi-localized (QL) phase between metal and Anderson insulator phases. In this QL phase, the localization length of wave functions diverges exclusively along the direction of the 1D weak topology. A comparison of the universal critical exponents across the three chiral symmetry classes indicates that the quantum criticality of the metal-to-QL transition varies, depending on the nature of the time-reversal symmetry. Motivated by this numerical observation, we revisit and extend our previous two-dimensional (2D) RG analysis to all three chiral classes, now newly incorporating a one-loop renormalization of the weak topological term in the analysis. The revised analysis reveals that a quasi-localized strong-coupling fixed point previously reported in the chiral unitary class is unstable under this new inclusion; instead, the strong-coupling phase is entirely governed by a stable fixed point with conventional localized character. Nevertheless, in the chiral unitary and chiral symplectic classes, the RG analysis still yields the hallmark of the 1D weak topology through the spatially anisotropic scaling of the Anderson transition criticality. These theoretical findings suggest that a conclusive numerical identification of the 2D QL phase requires a finite-size scaling approach that accommodates generic (anisotropic) spatial scaling.



[190]. [*Toward a Physics of Deep Learning and Brains*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22649 "Toward a Physics of Deep Learning and Brains")

Arsham Ghavasieh, Meritxell Vila-Minana, Akanksha Khurd, John Beggs, Gerardo Ortiz, Santo Fortunato

arXiv:2509.22649v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Deep neural networks and brains both learn and share superficial similarities: processing nodes are likened to neurons and adjustable weights are likened to modifiable synapses. But can a unified theoretical framework be found to underlie them both? Here we show that the equations used to describe neuronal avalanches in living brains can also be applied to cascades of activity in deep neural networks. These equations are derived from non-equilibrium statistical physics and show that deep neural networks learn best when poised between absorbing and active phases. Because these networks are strongly driven by inputs, however, they do not operate at a true critical point but within a quasi-critical regime -- one that still approximately satisfies crackling noise scaling relations. By training networks with different initializations, we show that maximal susceptibility is a more reliable predictor of learning than proximity to the critical point itself. This provides a blueprint for engineering improved network performance. Finally, using finite-size scaling we identify distinct universality classes, including Barkhausen noise and directed percolation. This theoretical framework demonstrates that universal features are shared by both biological and artificial neural networks.



[191]. [*Nonlinear Landau levels in the almost-bosonic anyon gas*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14679 "Nonlinear Landau levels in the almost-bosonic anyon gas")

Alireza Ataei, Ask Ellingsen, Filippa Getzner, Th\'eotime Girardot, Douglas Lundholm, Dinh-Thi Nguyen

arXiv:2510.14679v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We consider the quantitative description of a many-particle gas of interacting abelian anyons in the plane, confined in a trapping potential. If the anyons are modeled as bosons with a magnetic flux attachment, and if the total magnetic flux is small compared to the number of particles, then an average-field description becomes appropriate for the low-energy collective state of the gas. Namely, by means of a Hartree-Jastrow ansatz, we derive a two-parameter Chern-Simons-Schr\"odinger energy functional which extends the well-known Gross-Pitaevskii / nonlinear Schr\"odinger density functional theory to the magnetic (anyonic) self-interaction. One parameter determines the total number of self-generated magnetic flux units in the system, and the other the effective strength of spin-orbit self-interaction. This latter interaction can be either attractive/focusing or repulsive/defocusing, and depends both on the intrinsic spin-orbit interaction and the relative length scale of the flux profile of the anyons. Densities and energies of ground and excited states are studied analytically and numerically for a wide range of the parameters and align well with a sequence of exact nonlinear Landau levels describing Jackiw-Pi self-dual solitons. With increasing flux, counter-rotating vortices are formed, enhancing the stability of the gas against collapse. Apart from clarifying the relations between various different anyon models that have appeared in the literature, our analysis sheds new light on the many-anyon spectral problem, and also exemplifies a novel supersymmetry-breaking phenomenon.



[192]. [*A chemical avenue to manipulate field-reentrant superconducting rivalries in infinite layer nickelates*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22026 "A chemical avenue to manipulate field-reentrant superconducting rivalries in infinite layer nickelates")

Haowen Han, Yusong Zhao, Yi Bian, Tong Ma, Wenlong Yang, Shaohua Yang, Binghui Ge, Hongliang Dong, Chuanying Xi, Ze Wang, Nuofu Chen, Tian Shang, Toni Shiroka, Zaher Salman, Jia-Cai Nie, Ho-Kwang Mao, Jikun Chen

arXiv:2511.22026v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Recently, preliminary magnetic field-reentrant superconductivity manifested in high-temperature (Tc) Eu-doped infinite-layer (IL) nickelates, beyond analogous discoveries exclusively in low-Tc systems. This evokes intriguing fundamental issues about potential quantum-phase boundary and criticality between unconventional superconductivity and field-reentrant-one, which are inexplicable owing to formidable challenges in growing IL-nickelates towards later-series rare-earths. Herein, we open up chemical avenues to enable effective growth of (RE1-yRE'y)1-xEuxNiO2 (RE/RE': Pr, Nd, Sm, Gd, Dy), giving rise to discoveries of RE-4f-related quantum competition between high-Tc and reentrant superconductivity. Robust magnetic-field-reentrant superconductivity with uniaxial anisotropy is observed at superconducting-dome boundaries, stemming from Eu2+-4f7 associated competition between magnetic-fluctuation promoted pairing and exchange-field interactions. Their quantum-criticality is further modulable via RE(RE')-magnetism, which either reinforces reentrancy or elevates Tc (40.1 K) with more robust critical-current-density (~266 kA/cm2 at 2 K) beyond Sr-/Ca-doped counterparts. Our synthetic route enables the establishment of an ideal platform via IL-nickelates for studying 4f-related unconventional superconductivity and quantum-criticality.



[193]. [*Dense granular rheology from fluctuations*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01907 "Dense granular rheology from fluctuations")

Benjamin M. Alessio, Matthew R. Edwards, Ching-Yao Lai

arXiv:2601.01907v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: A governing equation for the granular temperature of flows past the jamming point is proposed and validated term-by-term with discrete element simulation. We measure and construct models for transport coefficients that do not diverge near the jamming fraction. As a limiting case of our equation, we derive the granular fluidity equation and find disagreement between the preexisting parametrizations of nonlocal granular fluidity, extended kinetic theory, and our discrete element simulations that is resolved by our continuum model.



[194]. [*Resolving the energy alignment between methylammonium lead iodide and C60: an in-situ photoelectron spectroscopy study*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07755 "Resolving the energy alignment between methylammonium lead iodide and C60: an in-situ photoelectron spectroscopy study")

Alberto Garc\'ia-Fern\'andez, Karen Radetzky, Stefania Riva, Birgit Kammlander, Brian Rydgren, Evelyn Johannesson, Rahul Mahavir Varma, H{\aa}kan Rensmo, Ute B. Cappel

arXiv:2601.07755v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Understanding and controlling the energy level alignment at interfaces between lead halide perovskites and electron transport layers is crucial for optimizing perovskite-based devices such as solar cells. In this work, we investigated the energy level alignment of C60 on in-situ cleaved MAPbI3 single crystals in multiple repeat experiments using photoelectron spectroscopy aiming to resolve inconsistencies reported in earlier studies. Our results show that both materials remain chemically stable upon interface formation, in contrast to the strong reactions typically seen when metals are evaporated on perovskite surfaces. By analyzing the Pb 4f and C 1s binding energies in detail, we determined that C60 consistently exhibits a downward energy shift toward MAPbI3, which works against efficient charge extraction. The magnitude of this shift, however, varies between different sample positions, highlighting that small variations in sample surfaces can lead to significant differences in energetic alignment. These findings underscore the decisive role of surface chemistry on interfacial energetics, explain performance variability in perovskite devices, and demonstrate the need to control and accurately measure surface properties. Furthermore, the observed energetic alignment can explain why further interface modification by charge blocking layers or surface passivation is needed for optimized device efficiencies.



[195]. [*Linear Ensemble-Constrained Quantum Monte Carlo: From Dilute Fermi Gas to Flat-Band Ferromagnetism*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08552 "Linear Ensemble-Constrained Quantum Monte Carlo: From Dilute Fermi Gas to Flat-Band Ferromagnetism")

Tu Hong, Kun Chen, Xiao Yan Xu

arXiv:2601.08552v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We present a finite-temperature canonical-ensemble determinant quantum Monte Carlo algorithm that enforces an exact fermion number and enables numerically stable simulations of correlated lattice fermions. We propose a stabilized QR update that reduces the computational complexity from standard cubic scaling $O(\beta N^3)$ to linear scaling $O(\beta N N_e^2)$ with respect to the system size $N$, where $N_e$ is the particle number. This yields a dramatic speedup in dilute regimes ($N_e \ll N$), opening unbiased access to large-scale simulations of strongly correlated low-density phases. We validate the method on the dilute Fermi gas with onsite Hubbard interactions, observing the suppression of the fermion sign problem in the dilute limit. Furthermore, we apply this approach to a one-dimensional flat-band system, where the canonical ensemble allows for precise control over filling. We reveal a ferromagnetic instability at low temperatures in the half-filling regime. Our linear-scaling approach provides a powerful tool for investigating emergent phenomena in dilute quantum matter.



[196]. [*A probabilistic framework for irreversible kinetics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10763 "A probabilistic framework for irreversible kinetics")

Manas V. Upadhyay

arXiv:2601.10763v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: A probabilistic framework for irreversible kinetics that unifies forward-in-time evolution and inverse inference, while quantifying epistemic uncertainty over admissible histories, is proposed. The forward-in-time evolution and inverse inference formulations differ only through observations and how they constrain admissible histories. All histories are ranked using a Gibbs-type probability measure $\exp(-\mathcal{J}/\Theta)$, where $\mathcal{J}$ encodes constitutive physics and observations. $\Theta$ controls epistemic uncertainty over the unknown histories, and the measure is interpreted as a Bayesian posterior over histories. Maximizing this posterior is equivalent to simultaneous minimization of $\mathcal{J}$ over the admissible history space. The framework is then specialized to generalized standard materials (GSM), for which $\mathcal{J}$ is constructed from incremental energy--dissipation functionals. This specialization reveals an important difference between the history resulting from simultaneous minimization over entire histories generally differs from the one generated by sequential GSM updates. Specifically, the causal GSM history incurs a higher history-level cost than the one obtained by simultaneous minimization, indicating that enforcing causality carries an additional cost at the history level. This difference is shown through seven representative examples: viscoelasticity, viscoplasticity, rate-independent plasticity, gradient damage, conserved and non-conserved phase transformations, and coupled thermoelectric evolution. Finally, the finite-$\Theta$ formulation is demonstrated on an inverse problem with a non-convex free energy, where incomplete initial-state information is used to infer unobserved intermediate states and quantify uncertainty over admissible histories. As $\Theta$ decreases, the posterior concentrates towards minimum-$\mathcal{J}$ histories.



[197]. [*Thermal Decoherence and Population Transfer of MeV Channeling Electrons in Diamond*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16529 "Thermal Decoherence and Population Transfer of MeV Channeling Electrons in Diamond")

Tadas Paulauskas

arXiv:2602.16529v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Channeling radiation from MeV-regime electrons is governed by transitions between quantized transverse bound states, but experimental spectra are strongly modified by thermal diffuse scattering. To capture these open-system dynamics, a frozen-phonon multislice framework is combined with bound-state projection analysis to construct depth-dependent reduced density matrices in selected transverse manifolds. Beyond reproducing experimental channeling-radiation transition energies, this approach separates thermal population transfer, intra-manifold decoherence, and cross-manifold coherence loss. Applied to 16.9 MeV axial electron channeling in $\langle100\rangle$ diamond, the results show approximately exponential population decay from the initially occupied states, accompanied by strongly channel-dependent feeding among low-lying manifolds. Starting from a coherent superposition within the degenerate 2p manifold, stochastic symmetry breaking by thermal displacements drives the intra-manifold purity toward the maximally mixed limit, indicating rapid phase scrambling. Under 1s initialization, population transferred into the 2p and 3d manifolds remains internally close to maximally mixed, yet a weak residual 2p-3d cross-manifold coherence persists. This framework goes beyond static mean-field thermal broadening and provides a microscopic basis for evaluating population dynamics and coherence lifetimes in strongly quantized channeling-radiation systems.



[198]. [*Casimir-Polder energy landscape: Unipolarizable atom and ring*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20203 "Casimir-Polder energy landscape: Unipolarizable atom and ring")

Niranjan Warnakulasooriya, John Joseph Marchetta, Prachi Parashar, K. V. Shajesh

arXiv:2602.20203v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The Casimir-Polder interaction energy between a unipolarizable point atom and a unipolarizable dielectric ring has been limited, until now, to the case when the atom is confined on the axis of symmetry of the ring. We find the generalized analytical expression for any position of the atom relative to the ring in terms of complete elliptic integrals. This is aided by the construction of a class of integrals of a Jacobian elliptic function as a linear combination of complete elliptic integrals. Our expression for the interaction energy allows us to investigate the instability of the atom even for the equilibrium points which exists off the axis of symmetry.



[199]. [*Interaction and disorder effects on Cooper instability in two-dimensional fractional Dirac semimetals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22772 "Interaction and disorder effects on Cooper instability in two-dimensional fractional Dirac semimetals")

Hua Zang, Jing Wang

arXiv:2602.22772v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Employing a renormalization group analysis that allows for an unbiased treatment of competing physical ingredients, we systematically trace how the interplay between Cooper pairing and disorder scatterings governs the emergence or suppression of Cooper instability in the low-energy regime of fractional Dirac semimetals.In the clean limit, we find that the emergence of Cooper instability requires surpassing a finite interaction threshold $|\lambda_c|$, and depends sensitively on both the fractional exponent $\alpha$ and the transfer momentum $\mathbf{Q}=(Q,\phi)$. Specifically, bigger values of $\alpha$ enhance the tendency toward BCS instability. For $\alpha\in(0.001,0.61)$, the $(Q,\phi)$ parameter space separates into two distinct regions: Zone-\uppercase\expandafter{\romannumeral1}, where Cooper instability is suppressed, and Zone-\uppercase\expandafter{\romannumeral2}, where it is allowed. In the presence of disorders, we demonstrate that they can either promote or suppress Cooper instability. Disorder of type $\Delta_1$ or $\Delta_2$ enhances superconductivity by reducing the critical interaction threshold $|\lambda_c|$ and expanding the superconducting phase space (Zone-\uppercase\expandafter{\romannumeral2}). In sharp contrast, either $\Delta_0$ or $\Delta_3$ suppresses Cooper pairing by increasing $|\lambda_c|$ and shrinking the available phase space (Zone-\uppercase\expandafter{\romannumeral1}). Although Cooper instability can be enhanced when promotive disorders ($\Delta_1$, $\Delta_2$) coexist with a single suppressive disorder ($\Delta_0$ or $\Delta_3$), the suppressive influence of $\Delta_{0,3}$ generally dominates the promotive effects of $\Delta_{1,2}$ in the presence of all sorts of disorders. These results would be helpful for further studies of fractional Dirac semimetals and alike materials.



[200]. [*Dualities and Topological Classification of the $S=1$ Pyrochlore Spin Ice*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03852 "Dualities and Topological Classification of the $S=1$ Pyrochlore Spin Ice")

Sena Watanabe, Yukitoshi Motome, Haruki Watanabe

arXiv:2603.03852v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We derive dual descriptions of the trivial paramagnetic, $\mathbb{Z}_2$-deconfined, and $\mathbb{Z}_2$-confined U(1) Coulomb phases of the $S=1$ pyrochlore spin ice. In the monopole-free limit, the system can be effectively mapped onto 3D $XY$ and Ising loop-gas models depending on the spin anisotropy, which provides theoretical estimates for the phase boundaries, while a macroscopic flux vector classifies the topological sectors via geometric parity rules. Thermal unit monopoles permit endpoints of both directed spin loops and unoriented vacancy loops. The resulting relevant symmetry-breaking fields in the dual $XY$ and Ising descriptions predict rounded crossovers near the monopole-free critical points. Monte Carlo simulations support this monopole-induced rounding.



[201]. [*Unambiguous characterization of in-plane dielectric response in nanoconfined liquids: water as a case study*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00700 "Unambiguous characterization of in-plane dielectric response in nanoconfined liquids: water as a case study")

Jon Zubeltzu

arXiv:2604.00700v5 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The in-plane dielectric constant of nanoconfined water has attracted growing interest in recent years. Nevertheless, assigning a bulk-like dielectric constant to a molecularly thin film is convention dependent because it requires choosing an effective water width. We propose the in-plane 2D polarizability, $\alpha_{\parallel}$, as a macroscopic characterization of the in-plane dielectric response that avoids this choice. Using classical molecular dynamics simulations, we compute $\alpha_{\parallel}$ via two independent and consistent methods: based on fluctuation-dissipation theory, and from the induced dipole moment when water is placed in a capacitor. Our results provide the framework to quantify the in-plane dielectric response of polar liquids across simulations and experiments.



[202]. [*Topological Phase Transitions and Their Thermodynamic Fate in Arbitrary-$S$ Classical Pyrochlore Spin Ice*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04346 "Topological Phase Transitions and Their Thermodynamic Fate in Arbitrary-$S$ Classical Pyrochlore Spin Ice")

Sena Watanabe, Yukitoshi Motome, Haruki Watanabe

arXiv:2604.04346v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We develop a self-contained theoretical framework that classifies the topological phases and critical phenomena of classical pyrochlore magnets with arbitrary spin $S$, subject to competing exchange and single-ion anisotropies. We work first in the monopole-free (ice-rule) limit. In the small-$w$ regime, where the single-ion term favors low spin amplitudes, dualities reveal a dichotomy: Integer spins exhibit a continuous 3D $XY$ deconfinement transition, whereas half-integer spins remain in a $U(1)$ Coulomb liquid without any transition. In the large-$w$ regime, where $|S^z| = S$, the macroscopic flux is quantized to multiples of $2S$. Mapping the defect structure to loop gases, we prove that the ice rule is compatible with the modular $\mathbb{Z}_{2S}$ flux conservation at the vertex level if and only if $S \le 3/2$. For $S=3/2$, this maps the system to the three-state Potts model, whose symmetry-allowed cubic invariant drives a first-order transition. For $S \ge 2$, no discrete clock mapping exists; a decomposition of the partition function shows that the only processes sensitive to the discrete $\mathbb{Z}_{2S}$ structure carry exponentially small weight and correspond to irrelevant operators at the 3D $XY$ fixed point, so the deconfinement transition is of the 3D $XY$ type. Finally, incorporating thermal monopoles, we show that they act as a symmetry-breaking effective magnetic field that severs defect strings. Consequently, the continuous transitions are rounded into crossovers, whereas the first-order $S=3/2$ transition is predicted to survive at finite temperatures, terminating at a critical end point. Classical Monte Carlo simulations for $S$ up to $7/2$ corroborate the predicted crossovers at the accessible temperatures, which lie above the estimated critical end point, so the survival of the $S=3/2$ first-order transition remains to be tested directly.



[203]. [*Boundary R\'enyi oscillations in the open XX chain*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05356 "Boundary R\'enyi oscillations in the open XX chain")

Miguel Tierz

arXiv:2604.05356v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: For the ground state of the semi-infinite XX chain with Fermi momentum $k_F$, we derive the extracted $2k_F$ contribution to the R\'enyi entropy of its first $\ell$ sites at fixed $\alpha>1$. Its phase $\sin[(2\ell+1)k_F]$ matches the Friedel oscillation at the entangling bond and becomes the even--odd alternation at half filling. An exact Hankel determinant reduction and Riemann--Hilbert analysis give every term $\mathcal{L}^{-(2q-1)/\alpha}$ for positive integers $q$ with $2q-1<\alpha$, where $\mathcal{L}=4\ell\sin k_F$, with an $O(\mathcal{L}^{-1})$ error within this extracted component for $k_F$ away from $0$ and $\pi$. At integer $\alpha=p\ge2$, exact factorization gives a finite sum over all roots with error $O(\mathcal{L}^{-1-1/p})$. We also determine the $2k_F$ terms of $\log Z_\alpha(\phi)$, where $Z_\alpha(\phi)=\operatorname{Tr}(\rho_A^\alpha e^{i\phi Q_A})$, $\rho_A$ is the block's reduced density matrix, and $Q_A$ is its fermion number. At fixed nonzero flux $|\phi|<\pi$ with $2q-1\mp\phi/\pi\ne\alpha$, the two exponential terms have different decay powers. Exact factorization sharpens the integer result. These pointwise estimates do not justify projection to fixed charge. The uncharged sequence and the two leading charged terms agree with earlier generalized Fisher--Hartwig predictions. Numerical calculations at $p=4$ test the predicted subleading coefficients without an adjustable normalization.



[204]. [*Quantum matter is weakly entangled at low energies*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14143 "Quantum matter is weakly entangled at low energies")

Samuel J. Garratt, Dmitry A. Abanin

arXiv:2604.14143v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We construct upper bounds on entanglement entropies of many-body quantum states that have fixed energy expectation values with respect to local Hamiltonians. Our focus is on entanglement entropies of subsystems that make up approximately half of the full system. The upper bound on the von Neumann entanglement entropy is half the sum of the thermal entropies of two fictitious systems at the same temperature as one another, with an additional area-law contribution in some systems. The effective temperature is chosen such that the sum of the thermal energies of the two fictitious systems matches the constraint on the energy of the state in the original problem; at subextensive energies, this temperature decreases with increasing system size. Our upper bounds on R\'{e}nyi entanglement entropies take an analogous form. These inequalities show that, in systems that are frustration-free (FF) and whose zero-temperature subsystem thermal entropies scale with surface areas (rather than subsystem volumes), ground states are area-law entangled. For physical models of quantum matter, which have well-defined specific heat capacities (and are not necessarily FF), our bounds provide a way to convert this thermodynamic data into constraints on pure-state entanglement at both subextensive and extensive energies. We also show that our general upper bounds on half-system entanglement entropies are optimal, up to subleading corrections, in systems with reflection symmetry and above a subextensive energy threshold. A key application of our results is to constrain the entanglement of quantum states relevant to simulations of zero-temperature response functions, which have energies of order unity. We prove that, in wide varieties of FF systems in two dimensions and higher, the contributions of excitations to von Neumann entanglement entropy are subleading compared with the contribution from the ground state.



[205]. [*Emergence of Open Chemical Reaction Network Thermodynamics within Closed Systems*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14848 "Emergence of Open Chemical Reaction Network Thermodynamics within Closed Systems")

Benedikt Remlein, Massimiliano Esposito, Francesco Avanzini

arXiv:2604.14848v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Under which conditions do the dynamics and thermodynamics of open chemical reaction networks (CRNs), grounded on the notion of idealized chemostats that exchange selected species, emerge from underlying closed CRNs? While open CRNs provide the standard framework to describe out-of-equilibrium chemical systems, real systems are finite and ultimately relax to equilibrium, leaving the status of this description conceptually unresolved. Here, we address this fundamental question. We show that open-CRN behavior arises as an asymptotic regime of closed CRNs when two physically minimal and transparent conditions are met: a time-scale separation, whereby fast reactions effectively act as exchange mechanisms, and an abundance separation, whereby a subset of species behaves as chemostats with diverging chemical capacity. In this regime, both the stochastic dynamics and the thermodynamic structure - including local detailed balance, entropy production, and free-energy balance - emerge to leading order from the underlying closed CRN. We further predict that this regime persists up to a characteristic time proportional to the scale of the abundance separation, thereby providing an experimentally accessible criterion for estimating how long a finite closed system behaves as an open CRN. Our results apply to arbitrary stoichiometries. They show that chemostats need not be introduced as external idealizations, but instead arise as emergent thermodynamic structures within closed systems, providing a unified and physically grounded foundation for the nonequilibrium thermodynamics of CRNs.



[206]. [*Relaxation Dynamics of Superconducting/Normal Interfaces in the Intermediate State of a type-I superconductor*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17333 "Relaxation Dynamics of Superconducting/Normal Interfaces in the Intermediate State of a type-I superconductor")

Mengju Yuan, Yugang Zhang, Ying Zhu, Jingchun Gao, Aifeng Wang, Mingquan He, Jun-Yi Ge, Yisheng Chai

arXiv:2604.17333v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The dynamics of phase boundaries, such as superconducting/normal (S/N) interfaces in type-I superconductors, are typically obscured in conventional magnetic measurements, which are strongly influenced by surface barriers and global flux processes. Here, we employ the complex ac magnetostrictive coefficient as a sensitive probe of the bulk dynamics of S/N interfaces in the intermediate state of lead. The ac magnetostrictive coefficient exhibits an unusual complex response, with a negative real component and a positive imaginary component. Frequency-dependent measurements show that the magnitude of the real component decreases monotonically with increasing frequency, whereas the imaginary component first increases, reaches a broad maximum, and then decreases. In contrast, ac susceptibility displays a conventional Debye-type magnetic relaxation with a different phase relation between its real and imaginary components. We propose that the magnetostrictive response arises from ac-field-driven relaxation and rearrangement of metastable S/N domain configurations toward states of lower free energy. These results reveal a distinct interfacial relaxation channel in the intermediate state and establish the complex ac magnetostrictive coefficient as a sensitive probe of nonequilibrium S/N interface dynamics.



[207]. [*Data-Efficient Indentation Size Effect Mitigation in Steels Using Machine Learning and a Physics-Constrained Neural Network*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27775 "Data-Efficient Indentation Size Effect Mitigation in Steels Using Machine Learning and a Physics-Constrained Neural Network")

Radmir Karamov, Tagir Karamov

arXiv:2604.27775v4 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Shallow nanoindentation, needed for thin layers, individual phases and small volumes, gives hardness inflated by the indentation size effect (ISE). The Nix-Gao correction reduces this inflation but requires deep indentations of the same material, often impossible in such samples. We present a data-efficient workflow, trained on a few reference materials, that predicts a high-load reference hardness from a single shallow indentation. Seven models were trained on 703 indentations (5-350 mN) on three steels (2.0-6.5 GPa): Ridge regression, Random Forest, XGBoost, two feed-forward networks, and two physics-constrained neural networks (PCNNs) that apply a bounded learned correction q as H_app/sqrt(1+q). Over five seeds, models were tested on an external steel not used for training and indented at different loads, in leave-one-steel-out folds, and on fused silica, an out-of-domain material where all models failed. Random Forest and XGBoost, the best on the development set, transferred worst over all loads (MAPE 10.2 and 8.5%), whereas Ridge regression, the PCNNs and one feed-forward network reached 3.7-4.8%; with three training steels, development-set accuracy thus did not indicate accuracy on an unseen steel. From only the external steel's 3-15 mN indentations, the smooth (non-tree) models estimated the reference within 0.03-0.19 GPa on average. The PCNNs matched the linear baseline within the training hardness range and were the most accurate for a held-out steel softer than the training steels, but failed for a harder one. The workflow uses no steel-specific inputs and can be retrained for other material classes.



[208]. [*First-Principles Effective Mass in the Three-Dimensional Uniform Electron Gas*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.02294 "First-Principles Effective Mass in the Three-Dimensional Uniform Electron Gas")

Pengcheng Hou, Daniel Cerkoney, Zhiyi Li, Tao Wang, Xiansheng Cai, Lei Wang, Gabriel Kotliar, Youjin Deng, Kun Chen

arXiv:2605.02294v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The quasiparticle effective mass $m^*$ of the three-dimensional uniform electron gas (UEG) is a fundamental Fermi-liquid parameter whose value and density dependence have remained controversial for decades. Using renormalized perturbation theory with explicit counterterms, we determine $m^*$ in the metallic regime ($r_s \le 6$) from first principles by two complementary routes -- the self-energy and the forward-scattering four-point vertex via the $p$-wave spin-symmetric Landau parameter $F_1^s$ -- that agree within uncertainties at each density through sixth renormalized order. The resulting $m^*/m$ remains close to unity throughout the metallic regime, with a shallow non-monotonic density dependence -- a minimum near $r_s\approx 1$ followed by a gentle upturn -- reflecting the interplay of exchange and dynamical screening in the self-energy, and disfavoring strong monotonic suppression. This finding supports a physical picture for the metallic UEG in which dominant charge correlations are concentrated in nearly forward scattering and generate only a weak $F_1^s$ component.



[209]. [*Diversity-Aware Active Learning Builds Materials Datasets for Current and Future Objectives*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05104 "Diversity-Aware Active Learning Builds Materials Datasets for Current and Future Objectives")

Rafael Espinosa Casta\~neda, Ashley Dale, Hongchen Wang, Yonatan Kurniawan, Hao Wan, Runze Zhang, Adji Bousso Dieng, Kangming Li, Jason Hattrick-Simpers

arXiv:2605.05104v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Materials science data collection can be expensive, making dataset reuse and long-term utility important for future discovery. In practice, data collection often prioritizes properties of current interest while other properties are excluded from the acquisition process. This can produce datasets that are well suited for current objectives but lose information needed for properties of future interest. Here, we present how to construct datasets that remain informative for both. We develop and evaluate acquisition frameworks that combine predictive disagreement with diversity in material features, predicted outcomes, or both. We test these approaches across four DFT datasets and an experimental thermoelectric dataset. Diversity inclusion in dataset construction can reduce present and future objectives trade-off by preserving a broader range of materials and property behavior during acquisition. Across all datasets, the Non-dominated Sorting Genetic Algorithm II with disagreements and feature diversity (NSGA-II Dis. + diversity) framework developed in this work provides the most robust balance between current and future objectives. It maintains prediction performance above random sampling for current objectives while mitigating bias on excluded properties and, in many cases, improving their performance beyond random sampling. This diversity-aware acquisition framework provides a practical route to building materials datasets with unbiased quality entries and mitigating cold-start limitations in subsequent modeling and discovery campaigns.



[210]. [*Singular Behavior of Observables at Hopf Bifurcations*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05194 "Singular Behavior of Observables at Hopf Bifurcations")

Benedikt Remlein, Massimiliano Esposito

arXiv:2605.05194v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Hopf bifurcations are a universal route to self-sustained oscillations in driven systems. Despite the absence of any singular stationary state, we show that time-averaged observables generically exhibit singularities at the onset of oscillations. The origin of this behavior is geometric: phase averaging over the emergent periodic attractor eliminates odd powers of the oscillation amplitude, while the squared amplitude varies smoothly with the distance from the bifurcation. Consequently, the excess of a time-averaged observable admits an integer-power expansion; observables remain finite but display discontinuities in finite-order derivatives. This yields an Ehrenfest-like hierarchy of Hopf singularities, in which the first nonanalytic derivative is determined by the lowest-order coupling between the observable and the limit-cycle waveform that survives phase averaging. The time averages of generic observables therefore exhibit kink singularities, while symmetry or geometric cancellations can suppress lower-order couplings and shift nonanalyticity to higher derivatives. We demonstrate this mechanism in chemical, electronic, and climate oscillators. Our results identify supercritical Hopf bifurcations as a universal mechanism for nonanalytic observable behavior, where singular yet non-diverging features emerge without any underlying singular stationary state.



[211]. [*Algebraic Structure of Finite-Size Bethe Solutions in the Heisenberg Chain*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05589 "Algebraic Structure of Finite-Size Bethe Solutions in the Heisenberg Chain")

Oliver R. Bellwood, William J. Munro

arXiv:2605.05589v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The spin-$1/2$ Heisenberg antiferromagnetic chain is the canonical example of an integrable quantum many-body model. Despite its exact solvability, explicit finite-size solutions are typically only accessible via numerical evaluation of the Bethe ansatz equations. Here, we analyse the algebraic structure of the exact, symbolic ground states for chains up to twelve sites using the Bethe ansatz. We find that the algebraic degree of the ground state magnon phases and Bethe roots scale with respect to system size at a different rate to the ground-state energy and wavefunction coefficients. The ground-state Bethe roots and energy are found to become Galois unsolvable for ten or more sites, thus demonstrating the lack of a symbolic analytical solution in a quantum integrable model due to algebraic complexity.



[212]. [*Opto-criticality in Drude metals near the plasma frequency*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08107 "Opto-criticality in Drude metals near the plasma frequency")

Bikram Keshari Behera, Rhitabrata Bhattacharyya, Shyamal Biswas

arXiv:2605.08107v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Within a simple unified framework of classical electrodynamics that incorporates background bound charges and currents, we have analytically determined the attenuation constant, phase constant, and reflectivity of Drude metals across the full frequency spectrum ($0 < \omega < \infty$).Our result for reflectivity agrees well with existing experimental data for both plasmas and seawater. Notably, for highly conductive Drude metals ($\omega_p \tau \gg 1$), the attenuation constant simplifies to $k_- \simeq \sqrt{\frac{\mu \epsilon}{2}} \sqrt{\omega_p^2 - \omega^2 + \vert{}\omega_p^2 - \omega^2\vert{}}$ across a broad high-frequency range spanning the plasma frequency $\omega_p$. The branch-point singularity at $\omega=\omega_p$ in this relation reveals opto-critical behavior near $\omega=\omega_p$ in properties such as the attenuation constant, group velocity, and complex dielectric constant, for which we have determined the corresponding opto-critical exponents. Furthermore, we have incorporated quantum corrections into these optical properties via the Drude-Sommerfeld model with Thomas-Fermi screening.



[213]. [*Landau theory applied to antiferroelectric ordering in ferroelectric nematic liquid crystals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11413 "Landau theory applied to antiferroelectric ordering in ferroelectric nematic liquid crystals")

Manisha Badu, Arjun Ghimire,  Milon, Priyanka Kumari, Hari Krishna Bisoyi, Oleg D. Lavrentovich, James Gleeson, Antal Jakli, Samuel Sprunt

arXiv:2605.11413v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The polarization and mass density modulation associated with antiferroelectric ordering in two ferroelectric nematic liquid crystals, a prototypical single compound (referred to as DIO) and a commercial mixture (FNLC919), are studied experimentally as a function of temperature. The wavenumber of the polarization modulation, qA, is determined by small angle X-ray diffraction from the weak, accompanying density wave (wavenumber qS = 2qA) using a conventional laboratory source. Combined results for qS and the saturated value of the polarization are analyzed in terms of Landau theory previously developed to describe the para-/antiferro-/ferroelectric sequence of phase transitions in solid ferroelectrics. The analysis of qS indicates that the polarization modulation can be reasonably well approximated by a simple sinusoid in DIO, whereas in FNLC919 a sharp decrease in qS close to the antiferro- to ferroelectric transition indicates development of a soliton-like profile.



[214]. [*AI estimation of partition functions with the PULSE method for the thermodynamic properties of disordered compounds*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28594 "AI estimation of partition functions with the PULSE method for the thermodynamic properties of disordered compounds")
